#2874. What Wall Street billionaires are saying about Democrats' midterm sweep

America's billionaires woke up Wednesday to a political reckoning — and a Wall Street rally.

As Democrats celebrated a sweeping midterm victory, some of the country's wealthiest donors traded barbs over record election spending. Others warned the results could threaten the artificial intelligence boom.

Wall Street, however, cheered the outcome. The Dow Jones Industrial Average soared 1,569 points, or 3%, while the S&P 500 gained 1.6% and the Nasdaq Composite rose 1.4%, as investors welcomed political gridlock, reduced uncertainty and President Donald Trump's willingness to accept the results.

Virgin Group founder Richard Branson took aim at the billions spent influencing the election.

"I have felt embarrassed by the billions that tech entrepreneurs and hedge fund managers have spent," Branson wrote Wednesday on LinkedIn, calling for campaign finance reform. Branson did not give donations that met the threshold reporting in the 2026 election cycle.

His comments came as Elon Musk, the election's largest individual donor, watched his preferred Republicans lose the Texas Senate and governor races despite pouring $224 million into the state — nearly half his record $474 million in political giving nationwide, according to a CNBC analysis of campaign finance records.

Early Wednesday, the Tesla and SpaceX CEO suggested on X that data centers in space were looking increasingly appealing before joking that his "election-interfering space lasers" had failed.

LinkedIn co-founder Reid Hoffman, another major political donor, took a sharper tone.

"We should get money out of politics," Hoffman wrote late Tuesday on X, accusing Musk of spending "outrageous amounts to keep authoritarian clowns in power" while defending his own contributions to Democrats.

Other Democratic megadonors celebrated. "The world changed yesterday," Alex Soros wrote Wednesday on X, hailing the results as a generational victory after his family contributed roughly $103 million to Democratic political efforts.

But beyond the political sparring, some of the country's richest investors were focused on a potentially bigger consequence of the Democratic sweep: the future of the AI boom.

Even before polls closed Tuesday, Pershing Square founder Bill Ackman warned on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" that slowing AI development could jeopardize America's technological edge. "Practically speaking, we can't slow down," he said.

As results rolled in Tuesday night, billionaire investor Chamath Palihapitiya sounded an even starker warning.

Palihapitiya initially questioned whether the early results would hold, but warned that if they did, the implications for the AI trade could be severe.

"And the only way to sink the AI trade is to sink the data centers," Palihapitiya said during an election-night livestream of the "All-In" podcast.

Texas offered an early test of those fears. Democratic Gov.-elect Gina Hinojosa, who has proposed a moratorium on new data-center construction until stronger safeguards are established, defeated three-term Republican incumbent Greg Abbott.

During the same livestream, Altimeter Capital CEO Brad Gerstner warned the Democratic wave could mean at least 10 to 15 gigawatts of expected AI data-center capacity would not come online in 2027. Wall Street analyst Dan Ives estimated Wednesday that 10% of planned U.S. data centers would no longer go ahead.

AMD CEO Lisa Su acknowledged the growing resistance to AI's expansion, urging technology companies to demonstrate the benefits of data centers to local communities.

"At the end of the day, what we need to do is ensure that everybody wins from AI," Su told CNBC Wednesday.

Yet Wall Street may have found opportunity in the same slowdown that worried AI's biggest backers.

Fewer new data centers could increase the value of existing facilities while easing the enormous spending burden on technology giants. Big Tech stocks surged Wednesday, adding more than $750 billion in market value at one point, even as smaller, debt-dependent data-center operators tumbled.

#2873. Reviewing the Bears’ PFN grades from Week 8 vs. Seahawks

The Bears made an impact in primetime on Monday night, decisively beating the Seahawks 34-17 to improve their record to 6-2 for the 2026-27 NFL season. It was a convincing win which saw Chicago excel on both sides of the ball, limiting the Seahawks' offense and gashing apart what is generally seen as one of the best defenses in the NFL. There were a lot of positive takeaways out of the All-22, and the analytics reflect that.

Each week, I'll review how Chicago's eligible performers graded by Pro Football Network's Impact Scoring model. Now that the rest of the NFL has caught up, and the numbers have all been crunched, here's how Week 8 looked.

Caleb Williams was an above-average performer at quarterback across the board on Monday night, which is made more impressive considering the quality of the Seahawks' defense. He graded in at least the top 12 in such categories as EPA per dropback, success rate, adjusted net yards per attempt, completion percentage, completion percentage over expected, and passing yards.

It helped that he had three efficient weapons to throw to; though Luther Burden III led all Bears weapons in PFN Impact grading, it was Rome Odunze who had the highest EPA per target, ranking fourth in the metric at +1.07. Likewise, Chicago had two top-ten performances at guard for the week out of both Jonah Jackson and Joe Thuney.

Jeffery Simmons made his Bears debut on Monday, and he absolutely shined on the big stage. His six tackles tied for the most on the team and tied for third among 92 qualified defensive tackles, and he also added a sack into the mix, despite facing a heavy double-team focus from Seattle's offensive line. The two biggest benefactors from Simmons' presence in Week 8 were Montez Sweat and Devin Bush, as both ended up with sacks, and the latter tied Simmons for the Bears' lead in tackles for the night.

The Seahawks picked on Tyrique Stevenson often, as he was targeted ten times and gave up a targeted passer rating of 124.2 for the game. However, strong auxiliary performances from Cam Lewis, Austin Booker, James Lynch, and D'Marco Jackson gave Chicago an all-around solid defensive outing.

Cairo Santos hit all of his field goal and extra point attempts, nailing his two field goal tries from 44 and 38 yards, respectively. His +0.4 field goals made over expected tied for the fourth best in the NFL in Week 8. On the other end of special teams, Tory Taylor finished above average with a 45.0-yard net punt average, including the lucky bounces he got on the one punt that got tipped.

#2871. Russia, China may be more aggressive if they feel Trump is on "his way out": Brookings

Russia and China could become more aggressive over the next two years if they conclude that President Donald Trump is politically weakened and on "his way out" after Democrats won control of both chambers of Congress, Brookings Institution senior fellow Robert Kagan told CNBC.

Speaking on "Squawk Box Asia," Kagan said the war with Iran had also exposed how Washington has "proved itself rather incapable militarily" in terms of capacity, which would be critical in Asia.

"Everybody can see how much our stocks are depleted in terms of interceptors and missiles, which are of vital interest in the Asian theater," he said.

Congressional Budget Office reports have estimated that the conflict depleted as much as two-thirds of U.S. missile-defense interceptors since June 2025, largely because of efforts to defend Israel against Iranian attacks. This could leave the U.S. with constrained inventories for several years.

Kagan said the deployment of three U.S. aircraft carriers to the Middle East had reduced the U.S. naval presence available in Asia, potentially giving China more room to act assertively in the region.

"I think Trump's only interest right now is in cutting economic deals with China. I don't think he has any real interest in confronting China, but that is going to lead China to be even more aggressive," he said.

In December, Trump approved an $11 billion arms package for Taiwan, but later held back on a second package worth about $14 billion, calling it a "bargaining chip" in talks with China.

China claims self-governing Taiwan as its territory. While Washington does not have a mutual defense treaty with the island, U.S. law requires it to provide Taiwan with the means to defend itself.

Former CIA Director William Burns said in 2023 that Chinese President Xi Jinping had instructed the military to be ready to invade Taiwan by 2027, while stressing that this did not mean Xi had decided to launch an invasion that year or at all.

Kagan's comments come as U.S., China and Russia discuss a possible trilateral summit between Trump, Xi and President Vladimir Putin at the APEC Leaders' meeting in Shenzhen later this month.

Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson Mao Ning said Wednesday that Beijing was "in communication with relevant parties regarding a meeting between the three heads of state," although no agenda has been announced.

Putin had reportedly floated the idea of a three-way summit with Trump and Xi in October to discuss ending the war in Ukraine, without Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy at the table.

"If it's clear that Donald Trump is on his way out, and the United States may be returning to something like normal, I don't want to overstate that, but they may feel both of them, both Russia and China, may feel that the time to move is in the next two years," Kagan said.

#2869. Coinbase net income surges 66% in third quarter, benefiting from bitcoin investment gains

Coinbase Global earned third-quarter adjusted net income of $719 million, up 66% from $433 million a year ago, largely due to a recovery in the cryptocurrency market.

Coinbase said it earned $2.51 a share in the latest quarter, although it wasn't immediately clear if that compared with the 6-cent-per-share profit that Wall Street analysts had estimated. Revenue of $1.20 billion trailed the $1.233 billion expected by analysts polled by LSEG.

Net income at the San Francisco-based platform surged thanks to the value of its volatile cryptocurrency investment portfolio, which can swing wildly even when the composition of the underlying holdings are little changed.

The third quarter value of Coinbase's crypto investment portfolio climbed due to a resurgence in the digital asset market. The price of bitcoin — often used as a gauge for all crypto assets — rose 39% in the three-month period ended Sept. 30 and the flagship cryptocurrency recently changed hands at roughly $80,809.

In the third quarter, Coinbase deepened its push into non-crypto products and services, including artificial intelligence integrations for traders, tokenized real-world assets and prediction markets.

In July, VP of product Max Branzburg told CNBC that Coinbase would debut an "everything exchange" spotlighting tokenized stocks and other real-world assets, derivatives trading, prediction markets and early-stage token sales. The trading platform also introduced Coinbase for Agents in early October, supporting users that want to use ChatGPT, Muse or other AI agents to manage their portfolios or research and trade various assets.

Coinbase also expanded its prediction market offerings, making a wider range of event contracts available to users.

In the third quarter, prediction markets revenue grew to more than $200 million on an annual basis, while the number of traders on those markets increased 60% compared with the second quarter.

#2868. What was really behind Wednesday's monster rally? Trump quickly making peace

The stock market skyrocketed on Wednesday on the heels of a decisive sweep for Democrats in the 2026 midterm elections. But that was not necessarily the only driver of the climb.

"President Trump's acknowledgement of the Democrats' victory in the midterms and his expressed openness to collaborating with the opposition have helped markets to rally on hopes that contestation of the results and more adverse polarization can be avoided," said Thomas Hempell, Generali Investments senior economist.

Trump told The New York Times and The Wall Street Journal overnight Tuesday that he accepted the results and would work with Democrats. Those headlines coincided with a rebound in stock market futures.

In the lead-up to this year's midterms, some on Wall Street grew increasingly concerned about the prospect of Trump contesting the election results. The Department of Justice's Alaska election lawsuit led to fears over delays with the outcome that would exacerbate the uneasiness in the market that's typically removed once the elections conclude.

CNBC's Fed Survey showed that among 30 fund managers, strategists and economists, 63% believe that the extended uncertainty around the balance of power created by legal challenges to the election results posed "some" market risk. That was a slight increase from 61% in September. Additionally, 14% believed it represented "significant" market risk, which was up from 7%.

Now, investors are breathing a sigh of relief over Trump's acceptance of the results, which should "act as a break on extreme policy outcomes and bring down risk premia," so long as the president's decision remains, said George Saravelos, head of FX Research at Deutsche Bank.

Trump has a history of rallying markets, as well as driving them lower, with his announcements. Jay Woods of Freedom Capital Markets, who noted that the rebound in stocks overnight was partially due to the president's "initial tone and reaction to the results," said the focus will shift to whether Trump's latest move will actually have staying power.

"I think Trump is going to try to do and say the right thing, but at two in the morning, he may change his mind," the chief market strategist said. "He is an X factor."

Woods added that he's "encouraged" by Wednesday's massive rally, but he noted that there are still headwinds that could plague the market over the coming weeks — such as concerns about inflationary pressures as a result of higher energy prices from the conflict in the Middle East.

"How Trump reacts to it will help us navigate those headwinds," he said.

Osaic's Philip Blancato said he believes that if Trump "continues to play nice" with Democrats, the market is going to have a "very strong" end to the year.

Stocks historically tend to rally after the midterms anyway, but this time around, some were worried the solid run-up in the S&P 500 this year was going to potentially hinder those gains. After Trump's reaction, Blancato said he doesn't think the S&P 500 hitting 8,000 by year-end is totally out of the question and that there's "a lot predicated on him, but not completely."

The broad market index gained more than 1% on Wednesday, with its closing price implying a roughly 4% gain to that level.

However, investors should be prepared for any future volatility if Trump's rhetoric does not remain the same, the chief market strategist said.

"The president's always going to be a wildcard," he said. "Let's not let one day make a year. This is an instant reaction to this thing. There's still plenty of things to be hashed out."

#2867. 49ers defense receives great news as Nick Bosa is suddenly ‘ready to go’

Reinforcements continue to come on the way for the San Francisco 49ers.

A day after wide receiver Deebo Samuel was seen working out at the team's practice facility in Santa Clara, the 49ers saw Nick Bosa back in action as a limited participant during Wednesday's practice.

Nick Bosa returns to practice on Wednesday as a limited participant. Getty Images

Bosa has been out since September 24. after he pulled his calf muscle during a team practice.

According to ESPN NFL reporter Nick Wagoner, Bosa isn't concerned about reaggravating his injury, as he said he feels "great" and is "ready to go."

Bosa, 29, has appeared in only two games for San Francisco and has seven tackles etched to his name.

The 49ers are coming off a bye week after suffering their first loss of the season in Week 7 to the Atlanta Falcons, where the San Francisco defense gave up 29 points, while the offense put up 20.

Bosa told David Lombardi of the SF Standard that "It's been tough," not being with his teammates the past six weeks.

"The team obviously had my back and held it down. We're getting some guys back this week so it's a long year and I kind of reminded myself that I need to be ready for the long haul and I think I'll be just that," Bosa said.

49ers' Gracen Halton celebrating with teammate Khalid Kareem during a Monday Night Football game against the Commanders. AP Photo/Kelley L. Cox

As Bosa is nearing a potential return this week, it also comes at the same time that his former 2019 teammate, DeForest Buckner, is set to return to the 49ers defensive line — a week after San Fransico traded for him from the Indianapolis Colts.

Buckner was in his fourth year in the NFL and lined up alongside Bosa during his rookie year in 2019.

The two combined for 16.5 sacks that year and went on to play in Super Bowl 54, where they lost to the Kansas City Chiefs.

Bosa was asked about the team trading for Buckner and said he was "very happy."

"I mentioned it to Kyle [Shanahan] a couple months ago, jokingly, but the fact that it actually happened, it's a full-circle moment for sure." Bosa said.

The former Defensive Player of the Year missed most of last season due to a torn ACL he suffered during their third game of the season.

Bosa coming back to the football field will come at the same time the 49ers welcome a former Bay Area rival, the Las Vegas Raiders, who are off to a surprising 4-4 start to the season and are coming off a 38-33 road victory against the New York Jets.

Aside from Bosa coming back, Wagoneer reported that Shanahan gave some good news about Mike Evans who's exited Week 6 early with an Achilles injury.

"I think we've made a lot of progress. I think he's going in the right direction and it's something we definitely got to be smart with, but we're not going to overly do it," Shanahan said. "We just want to wait till he's healthy and he's still working his way to get there."

Wagoner also reported that Christian McCaffrey was surprised when he injured his hamstring during the Atlanta game, but noted he could also return to the field this week.

"It wasn't too bad," McCaffrey said. "I think having the bye week was awesome, but I feel great."

#2861. Adam Silver reportedly would like to see NBA games shorten to 40 minutes

The 2026-27 NBA season has started, and we're already excited to see the Washington Wizards usher in a new era. But apparently, while the AJ Dybantsa Age is just starting, there's a new report that at some point in the future, you may not be spending as much time at Capital One Arena to watch a game like in the past.

According to Chris Mannix of Sports Illustrated, NBA Commissioner Adam Silver would like to see games shorten to 40 minutes.

Lowered risk of injury to players. Presumably, if players, especially starters, appear for fewer minutes a game, they will have fresher legs toward the end of the game.

Fewer minutes, but not necessarily fewer games: NBA fans of the future can compare to the current era and the 1990s more easily when it comes to team records.

The NBA will meet the FIBA standard length: FIBA games played in Europe, Asia, Africa and elsewhere in the Americas are 40 minutes long with 10 miunte quarters. The WNBA also plays games based on this standard length.

TV time: When you watch a Washington Mystics game where Sonia Citron, Kiki Iriafen and Shakira Austin beat the Indiana Fever and their ballyhooed roster, the game fits a bit more neatly in a two-hour window including timeouts. A Washington Wizards game where Dybantsa, Anthony Davis and Trae Young beat the Indiana Pacers is more like 2 hours and 30 minutes with a 48 minute game length. If they play two hours with a 40 minute game, perhaps the Big Three will be at a collective lower risk of injury. If TV timeouts were more tightly controlled like an international tournament (AmeriCup, the World Cup or Olympics), games can regularly be a bit under 2 hours each.

Potentially less time for bench players. If there are 82 games at 40 minutes each without overtimes, there would be 656 minutes left than an NBA regular season with 82 games at 48 minutes each. That's the equivalent of 13.67 games at 48 minutes a game and 656 fewer minutes, not including overtime, to give to players, especially bench players. Also, superstars and starters who are averaging over 30 minutes in games with 12-minute quarters may not see much of a drop-off in playing time if games suddenly had 10 minute quarters.

Career statistical marks will be harder to achieve by younger and future NBA players. Certain records, like the career scoring record with LeBron James holds (and it's still increasing) will be even more difficult to reach.

Would you like to see NBA games align with the 40 minute FIBA standard? Or do you think the time limit should stay at 48 minutes? Let us know in the comments below.

#2856. $6.3 billion Binance hack is largest crypto theft on record

Binance said Friday that a security incident impacted about $6.3 billion in client assets, with around $3 billion frozen on-chain, marking the largest crypto hack on record.

The company later added it began recovering assets impacted by the breach after pledging to cover losses using its Secure Asset Fund for Users, which the company created in 2018 as a protection for clients in the event of a hack.

It wasn't clear how the attack took place. The world's largest crypto exchange initially said the hack may involve "a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop, which allowed an attacker to gain access to a service container and submit unauthorized withdrawal transactions." However, Co-CEO Richard Teng later said the attack involved a more complex process rather than the misuse of a single engineer's permissions.

The FBI said Friday it was aware of the incident and was in touch with "relevant public and private partners to understand the scope of the activity, evaluate ​potential impacts, ​and ⁠coordinate appropriate investigative steps."

It wasn't clear either who was responsible for the breach. But TRM Labs, a blockchain intelligence and AI investigations company, pointed to North Korean hackers as the potential culprits. "Several features of this attack match earlier North Korea-linked heists, including the Bybit hack and the KelpDAO hack in April 2026: small Tornado Cash withdrawals funding an operator wallet, staging that began well before the theft, and signatures obtained in advance then executed in one burst," the company said in a post.

Blockchain data platform Chainalysis also said this appears "be the work of North Korean cybercrime group TraderTraitor. This group has repeatedly targeted the cryptocurrency industry, exchanges, DeFi protocols, and investors, to raise funds for the regime. We base our intelligence assessment on on-chain activity observed in the hours following the hack."

Binance temporarily suspended withdrawals while it overhauls its withdrawal security systems and has revoked the affected address authorizations, Teng said. The exchange is also working with law enforcement and blockchain intelligence firms to trace and recover the stolen funds, while deposits remain unaffected.

Binance co-founder Yi He said deposits remained unaffected and continued to operate normally, while the exchange was working on enhancements to its withdrawal service and related address controls. It expects to begin resuming withdrawal services within the next 48 hours.

"We have found no evidence of any wallet private key leak. We will share a detailed technical post-mortem once the investigation is complete and the facts are fully verified," Binance said.

The announcement followed moves by several crypto firms, including Circle Internet Group, Tether and Ripple, to freeze funds tied to the hack.

Exploits are not uncommon in the crypto space. In 2025, Bybit suffered roughly $1.5 billion in losses due to a hack. More recently, Bitget lost more than $350 million worth of digital assets in a cyber theft incident. Over the past several years, bad actors have also drained billions of dollars in crypto from decentralized exchanges.

This week's breach "proves our exact point in proposing regulations for crypto markets earlier this month," Michael Selig, chairman of the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, said Friday in a CNBC "Squawk on the Street" interview. The U.S. derivatives regulator on Oct. 5 proposed a framework that would effectively put crypto exchanges under federal government oversight — after Congress failed to approve legislation to regulate the asset.

"The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don't have the same sorts of safeguards that we have in our financial markets here in the United States. We want to offer a federal option to these exchanges to come and register with the CFTC, and they would be subject to the same sorts of cyber security safeguards that we have in our markets today," he said.

Bitcoin fell more than 4% to $77,187.60 on Friday as the hack weighed on crypto markets.

Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire said Thursday evening in a social media post that the company had frozen more than 800 million in USD Coin associated with the incident. USDT stablecoin issuer Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino also said in on X that his firm had frozen more than 1.5 billion of its stablecoin that may have been related to a potential breach.

Jack McDonald, Ripple's senior vice president of stablecoins, said it was actively freezing affected funds held in RLUSD, its U.S. dollar-backed stablecoin.

#2855. NFL odds, betting, storylines: 2 underdogs to back in Week 9

If the NFL betting community handed out a first-half MVP award, there is no question who this year's runaway winner would be: big NFL underdogs.

Through eight weeks, seven teams have taken the field as double-digit underdogs. Five of them covered the spread.

So far, we have seen 37 underdogs of six points or more (including those seven double-digit pups). Their ATS record: 24-10-3 ATS.

This includes a 6-2 ATS mark posted by the largest underdog of the week. In fact, the biggest underdog has covered the spread each of the last five weeks and recorded three outright upsets.

All of which brings me to my trio of NFL underdog best bets for Week 9. Spoiler alert: I expect big 'dogs to continue barking.

Malik Willis (No. 2) and Ollie Gordon II could have a surprise in store for the Lions. (Photo by Megan Briggs/Getty Images)

After an uneven 2-2 start to the season — a start that included a frantic overtime home win against the Saints, blowout road losses to the Bills and Panthers, and zero covers — the Lions have turned things around.

The bounce-back began with a 45-21 thumping of the Cardinals in Arizona. Detroit then followed its bye with consecutive divisional home wins over Green Bay and Minnesota. Those wins, however, were hardly convincing. The Lions needed a last-second field goal to upend the Packers 20-17 and a late-game touchdown and defensive stop to hold off Minnesota 19-15.

So seven games into the 2026 season, Detroit has defeated just two opponents by more than four points: Arizona and the New York Jets (who lost 31-24 in Motown in Week 3). The Lions also enter Week 8 having covered only two point spreads.

Now they head to South Beach in what is a classic letdown/sandwich spot.

The letdown part: Detroit just barely beat two NFC North rivals in tense, down-to-the-wire games (both of which were at home). Now with their confidence sky high, the Lions hop on a plane to face one-win Miami.

The sandwich part: Detroit next week kicks off a three-game homestand against New England. So this is the team's only road trip over a six-game stretch.

My point is that this is a tricky situation for the Lions. Which means it's an advantageous one for the Dolphins, who have been much more competitive over the past month.

Since opening with double-digit losses to the Raiders, 49ers and Chiefs by the combined score of 86-36, Miami has upset the Jets 28-25 on the road and held its own against the Vikings (15-10 road loss as a 10-point underdog), Bengals (31-24 home loss as a 7-point underdog) and Patriots (24-19 home loss as a 7-point underdog).

Yep, Miami comes into this contest on a 3-0-1 ATS run. And while the defense has admittedly struggled most of the season, the offense has picked it up recently. Since tallying 13, 13, 10 and 10 points in their first four games, the Dolphins averaged 23.7 points in the last three.

If the offense can exceed that average Sunday by a tiny bit — against a Detroit defense that I'm not convinced is fixed — Miami will get inside this big number.

In Week 1, the Commanders traveled to Philadelphia as 6-point underdogs. They lost 24-22.

Two weeks later, with injured starting quarterback Jayden Daniels on the sidelines, the Commanders hosted defending champion Seattle as 8.5-point underdogs. They won 33-31.

Fast-forward to Week 6, and Washington traveled to San Francisco as a 7.5-point underdog on Monday Night Football. The 49ers won — but only by six points (23-17).

Those were the three biggest point spreads the Commanders have faced as an underdog this season. And they covered them all. Now they're catching a bunch of points against the visiting Rams, who have looked much better the last two weeks, thumping the Raiders and Chargers by the combined score of 59-30.

But let's not forget that this same Rams squad preceded those two wins with an 18-16 loss to Arizona as a 12.5-point home favorite. Before that, L.A. suffered an overtime home loss to Buffalo; needed a furious late fourth-quarter rally to win at Philadelphia; and blew a 16-0 halftime lead in a 30-26 loss at Denver.

Back to Washington: True, it got crushed 37-20 at Dallas as a 4.5-point underdog in Week 2. But it lost Daniels to an elbow injury right before halftime.

The Commanders also fell 30-13 to the Colts in London as 5-point underdogs in Week 4. But seventh-round rookie quarterback Athan Kaliakmanis played the majority of that game after backup Marcus Mariota went down early.

Daniels finally returned the following week, which was the now-infamous 27-27 tie against the Giants (who are 4-2-1). Then came the competitive loss at then-unbeaten San Francisco, followed by an off week, followed by last Sunday's 27-10 rout of the Eagles.

Bottom line: The Commanders have been blasted only twice this season — once with their backup QB playing the second half and once with their third-string rookie QB playing the majority of the game. Assuming Daniels can stay on the field for 60 minutes Sunday, I like Washington's chances to hang with a West Coast team traveling across the country to play in the early window.

By the way, the Rams' 14-point victory at Las Vegas two weeks ago? It's the only time they've won by more than four points in their last eight games outside of Los Angeles.

#2853. Packers RB Josh Jacobs describes latest abuse allegations as an 'attack,' not worried about suspension from league

Green Bay running back Josh Jacobs addressed the new abuse allegations made against him for the first time on Wednesday afternoon.

Jacobs, who has returned to the field for the Packers in recent weeks, again denied any wrongdoing. He said the latest wave of abuse allegations, which came after his arrest earlier this year, felt like an "attack."

"It really felt like an attack on me and who I am and my character," Jacobs said, via NBC26's Kelly Hallinan. "At the end of the day, there's a lot of stuff that's being said about me that's completely false, like completely made up. People just run with it.

"I can't change that … I didn't want to go to a place where I felt like I was closed off and I'm angry all the time because of what's going on in my life."

Jacobs was first arrested in northern Wisconsin in May and charged with multiple counts of domestic abuse. He allegedly grabbed a woman who was trying to leave his garage and threw her to the ground.

Jacobs was formally charged with battery and criminal damage to property after that incident. He pleaded no contest to both charges. Jacobs landed on the commissioner's exempt list as a result of his arrest and missed the first six games of the season.

Last week, previously unreleased files from the Hobart/Lawrence Police Department revealed that they were contacted by at least three other women accusing Jacobs of abuse.

One woman told police that Jacobs slapped her in the face and took her phone as she tried to leave his home. She said Jacobs grabbed her by the hair and throat, pinned her down on the bed and threw her onto the floor. A second woman said Jacobs grabbed her arm, pushed her up against the wall and threw items of hers outside of the home.

A third woman said Jacobs flew her to his home and later grabbed her by the head, dragged her, scratched her chest and took her phone after it fell to the floor during an altercation. The woman said she had bruising on her arm and scratches on her chest, and that she didn't report it because she feared being deported from the United States.

Jacobs has denied any wrongdoing through his attorney, and said that they have "a substantial body of tangible evidence to back up his assertions."

"Everything that my lawyer said in the statement, it's one of those things where I'm going to lean on that," Jacobs said. "It's just one of those things where it's like, anything can be said and people are just going to run with it. I can't really change how you feel. But at the end of the day, I know what I have on my part."

Jacobs returned to the field for the Packers' loss to the Detroit Lions on Oct. 25, and he had 36 yards on 11 carries in that contest. He had 29 yards on 21 carries and scored a touchdown the following week in their win against the Carolina Panthers.

The NFL is investigating the new allegations made against Jacobs, and could still hand down a suspension. Packers head coach Matt LaFleur said Monday that they were letting the league process play out, but he expected that Jacobs would remain with the Packers.

Jacobs said he isn't expecting any further suspension, either.

"With all the evidence that we have and that they have, at this point it's not one of those things where I'm sitting back on my hands like, 'OK, am I going to play this week?' I kinda already know what it is," Jacobs said. "It's really just up to them to make their final decision. But I'm not worried about feeling like I'm going to miss any more time than I already have."

When Jacobs scored a touchdown against the Panthers, he celebrated in the end zone at Lambeau Field by appearing to mimic that his hands were handcuffed behind his back — something that has drawn criticism considering his recent arrest and the abuse allegations that were levied against him.

Jacobs again denied that the celebration had anything to do with his arrest, and said instead that he was referencing a gospel song.

He did, though, say he spoke with LaFleur and apologized for the extra attention that it's brought to the team.

"It wasn't my intention to make it a thing that it would be negative," Jacobs said. "The thing that I was receiving when he was walking to me was, it's not just me that has to answer for it … I told him for that, I apologize."

#2852. Milwaukee Bucks (1-6) at Boston Celtics (5-2) Game #8 11/4/26

Milwaukee Bucks (1-6) at Boston Celtics (5-2)Wednesday, November 4, 20267:30 PM ETTV: NBCSB, My24 Bucks+, League PassRadio: 98.5 Sports Hub, WTMJ 103.3 FM, Sirius XMRegular Season Game #8, Home Game #6TD Garden

The Celtics go for their 5th straight home win as they return home to host the Milwaukee Bucks. This is the first of 3 games between these two teams this season. They will play in Milwaukee on November 8 and again in Boston on April 11. The Celtics won the series last year 3-1, losing one game in Milwaukee. The Celtics are 124-113 overall, all time against the Bucks and they are 77-40 in games played in Boston.

The Celtics are 3rd in the East with only Philadelphia (7-1) and Detroit (6-1) ahead of them in the standings. The Bucks are 14th in the East with only Brooklyn behind them. The Celtics are 4-0 at home and 1-2 on the road. The Bucks only win this season was a home win against the Indiana Pacers. The Celtics have won the last 3 games against the Bucks.

The Bucks have overhauled their roster since last season. They made the big trade that sent Giannis Antetokounmpo and Bobby Portis to Miami for Tyler Herro, Kel'el Ware, Jaime Jaquez, Jr, and Kasparas Jakucionis. They also traded for Caris LeVert from Detroit and signed Pete Nance and Kam Jones to two way contracts. They signed Bogoljub Markovic as a free agent. They also drafted Braydon Burries with the 10th pick and Nate Ament with the 13th pick. They lost Thanasis and Alex Antetokounmpo, Andre Jackson, Kevin Porter along with Giannis and Porter. They also hired Taylor Jenkins as their head coach, replacing Doc Rivers.

After this game, the Celtics will head out on a road trip that will take them through Charlotte, Milwaukee, Philadelphia and Toronto. They play Dallas and Orlando at home before a West coast trip that takes them through Denver, Sacramento, Golden State, and Washington. They then play one game at home against Atlanta before playing at San Antonio and Dallas. Then it's home against Charlotte before a game at Brooklyn.

After this game, the Bucks will return home to host Toronto and Boston before playing at New York and Orlando. They then host Atlanta and play at Miami and host Portland. Then they play Philadelphia and Toronto on the road before hosting Brooklyn. Then it's one game at Detroit before a 3 game home stand against Oklahoma City, San Antonio and New York.

The Celtics have been changing their starting lineup with Jordan Walsh, Hugo Gonzalez, Baylor Scheierman, and Neemias Queta getting starts along with Derrick White, Paul George, Jayson Tatum and Mitchell Robinson. Against the Rockets, White, Scheierman, Gonzalez, Tatum and Queta got the start. The starting lineup is a work in progress for the Celtics and so it's hard to predict who Joe will go with from game to game.

I'm just guessing that White, Gonzalez, George, Walsh and Queta will start in this one. Since Tatum is out, it's possible that Scheierman once again starts but I'm guessing that Walsh will get the start. For the Bucks, they have started Burries, Herro, Jaquez, Kuzma and Turner in all 7 games so far and I'm going to guess that the same lineup starts this game.

The Celtics still have Devin Carter on the injury report due to illness. After missing the final 3 games of the preseason, Carter was diagnosed with mononucleosis and is without a timetable for a return. After playing both games in 2 back to back sets, Jayson Tatum is listed as out for rest. Payton Pritchard is also listed as questionable with a right shoulder contusion. Milwaukee has just Kam Jones (ankle) as questionable on their injury report at this time.

Probable Starting Matchups PG: Derrick White vs Brayden Burries

Celtics ReservesChris Cenac, JrMike Conley, JrLuka GarzaJordan WalshRon Harper, JrSam HauserPayton PritchardMitchell RobinsonBaylor Scheierman

Two Way PlayersDevin CarterDillon MitchellAmari Williams

Injuries/OutDevin Carter (illness) outJayson Tatum (rest) outPayton Pritchard (shoulder) questionable

Two-Way PlayersKam JonesPete NanceCorman RyanInjured/OutKam Jones (ankle) questionable

Key Matchups Hugo Gonzalez vs Tyler HerroHerro is in his first season in Milwaukee after being traded from Miami. He is averaging 21.9 points, 5.0 rebounds, and 4.0 assists. He is shooting 45.9% from the field and 43.1% from beyond the arc. He is a very good shooter and so the Celtics need to defend him well wherever he is on the court. I'm guessing that Hugo gets the start here again, but with Joe playing mad scientist with the lineups, you never know.

Paul George vs Jaime Jaquez, JrAs with Herro, Jaquez is in his first season with the Bucks. He is averaging 16.6 points, 7.3 rebounds, and 2.9 assists per game. He is shooting 50.6% from the field and 44.0% from beyond the arc. He is a good shooter and so, as with Herro, the Celtics need to stay with him and defend him well. Honorable MentionJordan Walsh vs Kyle Kuzma Kuzma is averaging 10.0 points, 6.4 rebounds, and 2.9 assists per game. He is shooting 43.9% from the field and 29.0% from beyond the arc. Although he is struggling from the perimeter, he is very capable of hitting 3's if left open. Tatum is out so the Celtics may start Hauser, or Scheierman or Walsh or any one of several other players here if Tatum doesn't play.

Keys to the GameDefense – Defense is always the biggest key to winning. Defense really does win championships. The Celtics are 11th with a defensive rating of 109.3. The Bucks are 26th with a defensive rating of 118.6. The Celtics must work hard on defense and keep their focus on the defensive end so as not to allow any easy baskets for the Bucks. They especially need to defend the perimeter as the Bucks are 3rd in the league attempting 43.7 three pointers per game.

Rebound – Next to defense, rebounding is always a big key to winning. The key to rebounding is effort and focus. The Bucks are 3rd with 49.6 rebounds per game while the Celtics are 20th with 42.4 rebounds per game. The Celtics especially need to fight for offensive rebounds as the Bucks are 2nd with 15 offensive rebounds per game while the Celtics are 13th with 12.6 offensive boards per game. The Celtics have to fight as a team to try to grab every rebound to avoid the Bucks getting 2nd chance points. The Bucks are 3rd, getting 19.0 second chance points per game.

Don't Underestimate the Bucks – The Bucks have not been playing well so far this season, going just 1-6 in their first 7 games. However, the Bucks are still a rival in the Eastern Conference that can play hard, which makes up for a lot of problems. The Celtics have to give max effort on the boards and on defense and they have to give that effort for the entire game and not just 1 or 2 quarters.

3 Point Shooting – The Celtics are 8th in the league, averaging 40.6 three pointers per game. They are shooting 34.5% as a team from beyond the arc. The Bucks are 3rd, averaging 43.7 threes a game and 20th in the league shooting 33.3% on threes as a team. Against the Rockets, no one on the Celtics could hit their 3's. If the Celtics' 3's aren't falling in this game, they need to take the ball inside.

X-FactorsHome Game – The Celtics are home once again after a 2 game road trip. They have the home crowd behind them to give them extra motivation. The Bucks are on the road for the 2nd straight game and have the distractions of travel and a hostile crowd. The Celtics need to take advantage of playing at home and play with energy and effort. The Celtics have not lost at home this season and the Bucks have not won on the road. They need to keep it that way.

Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game. The Celtics also have to be aware of the new points of emphasis for this season and try to avoid flopping, off‑arm push‑offs, reckless closeouts, delays before free throws, and illegal screens. They have been especially calling flops and push offs and so the Celtics need to especially avoid doing both.

#2851. UC Bearcats face another dangerous QB in Houston's Conner Weigman

The tour of dangerous Big 12 quarterbacks continues for the Cincinnati Bearcats as they visit No. 17 Houston on Saturday afternoon, Nov. 7, at TDECU Stadium.

The Bearcats have faced the scrambling abilities of Kansas State's Avery Johnson, the passing threat of Arizona's Noah Fifita, the unpredictability of West Virginia's Michael Hawkins Jr., Texas Tech's Will Hammond and Utah's dual-threat Devon Dampier. The Big 12 wins came against Kansas State with Johnson and Texas Tech with Hammond, while the escapability of Fifita, Hawkins and Dampier led to three Big 12 losses.

Next up is Houston's Conner Weigman, who was at Texas A&M two seasons ago. The 6-foot-3 native Texan is No. 14 in the nation in passing efficiency, ahead of Hawkins and Dampier, who gave UC fits. He is No. 8 in passing touchdowns with 20, more than Fifita and Dampier and seven more than UC's JC French IV. He's No. 20 in passing yards with 2,053, more than Dampier, who lit the Bearcats up for three passing touchdowns.

"Weigman does everything really, really good," UC Bearcats coach Scott Satterfield said. "He can make all the throws, he's sneaky fast, he can make safeties miss when he runs the football. They run the offense through him. He's the catalyst for their offense. They don't ever get themselves out of position."

Houston leads the nation in third-down efficiency at 60%, compared to UC at No. 33 at 44.6%.

It's all even, 15-15, but the Bearcats have won five straight. UC shut the Cougars out 34-0 in 2024, the first year for current coach Willie Fritz. The game before that in 2023 was in Houston, where the Bearcats recorded their first Big 12 win, 24-14 against the Dana Holgorsen-coached Cougars.

Houston's last win came at Nippert Stadium in 2016 as the Cougars rolled Tommy Tuberville's last UC team, 40-16.

Stream Cincinnati Bearcats vs.Houston Cougars

Why Cincinnati Bearcats have an advantage: It's another weekend in 2026 college football where one week you're the hammer, the next you're the nail. Coaches can go from the hot seat to the short list of another job based on a win, or vice versa based on a loss.

"There's a lot of parity in college football," Satterfield said. "Most of these teams have a new roster. What's the chemistry like? And there's a lot of up and downs throughout the season. Who's injured? Who's playing? Who's not playing? Where are you playing? Everybody's playing nine conference games now. It makes it exciting for the fans. This parity is what everybody wants. This year, in all these games, everybody's got a chance to win."

Should the Bearcats get center Taran Tyo and nose tackle Josh Hough back from injury, it would be a shot in the arm. Cole Tabb has run effectively, with three straight 100-plus-yard games; if Gi'Bran Payne can add to that and get UC over the 200-yard mark, it may help slow the Houston offense. Utah has been the only team to surpass 200 yards rushing on the Cougars, gaining 226 on Oct. 24. Outside of that, Texas Tech had 168 on the ground and those are Houston's only two losses.

Why Houston has an advantage: After losing at Utah 21-13 on Oct. 24, the Cougars had a bye week to heal up and scout the Bearcats. Plus, they're home. TDECU Stadium is not as intimidating a venue as others, but everyone loves a winner and Willie Fritz's men have only lost to Texas Tech and Utah, both ranked teams.

Like Cincinnati, Houston has NFL and MLB teams competing with it for fans. They also have the NBA. Arizona State and Colorado are in similar predicaments. Most of the Big 12 schools are primarily in college towns.

Houston averages 39,199 fans this season, about 7,000 more than last season and about 12,000 more than 2024. In UC's last visit in 2023, the Bearcats prevailed in front of 34,312.

It's also November and the Bearcats in the Big 12 are 1-11 in the 11th month of the year. However, the lone November win did come in UC's inaugural Big 12 season in Houston.

"Every year's different; we've got a completely different team," Satterfield said. "Not looking at exterior things and focusing on Houston is what we have to do. Then, you have to go out and play. Last year we played really good teams in November."

Based on this week's rankings, UC still has two ranked teams to play: at No. 17 Houston and at No. 7 BYU on Nov. 28. Iowa State won't be a picnic on the road Nov. 14, and there's a home date Nov. 21 vs. Colorado.

The Bearcats played without nose tackle Josh Hough and center Taran Tyo vs. Utah. Early indications are that both should be available for the Houston game.

Houston quarterback Conner Weigman left Rice-Eccles Stadium in Utah in a walking boot after their Oct. 24 loss. The Cougars were idle and based on Willie Fritz's press remarks Nov. 3, he's back up to speed.

The Big 12 announced Nov. 2 that the UC game at Ames vs. Iowa State is in a six-day window for TV. Kickoff should be known by Sunday, Nov. 8.

#2850. D.J. Reed, DJ Wonnum out of practice for Lions Wednesday

The Lions were obviously without linebacker Jack Campbell at practice on Wednesday and two other key defensive players were also on the sideline as the team worked to get ready to face the Dolphins.

Cornerback D.J. Reed (hip) and defensive end DJ Wonnum (concussion) were both out of action for the Lions. Both players were injured during last Sunday’s win over the Vikings.

Center Cade Mays (ankle) was also hurt in that game and he missed practice as well.

Safety Brian Branch (concussion), safety Kerby Joseph (knee), linebacker Amen Ogbongbemiga (toe), running back Jacob Saylors (neck), and wide receiver Amon-Ra St. Brown (rib) were all limited participants. Cornerback Rock Ya-Sin (cramps) was a full participant for the Lions.

#2849. The midterm election could reshape the muni bond market. Here’s what investors need to know

The outcome of Tuesday's election is expected to have broad implications across the market, including municipal bonds.

The spotlight has been on the battle for congressional control and whether Democrats will wrest the House of Representatives, the Senate or both from Republicans. But for the muni market, what happens at the state level could have bigger repercussions — at least in the near term.

There is an array of initiatives on ballots around the nation that could change tax laws and add to bond supply. Taxes matter to muni investors since the income received is free of federal tax, as well as state tax if the holder lives in the state where the bond is issued.

Depending on the location, ballot measures are up for approval that would either provide tax breaks, or raise taxes on the wealthy.

"It's a complete bifurcation between red and blue states," said Dan Close, head of municipals at Nuveen. "Blue states are trying to put in place graduated income tax structures, trying to capture more revenue from high-income earners. Red states are promoting affordability by trying to cut property taxes."

Red states are those that predominantly vote Republican, while blue states swing toward Democrats.

For instance, California has a proposition known as the "billionaire tax," which would impose a one-time levy equal to 5% of the wealth of certain taxpayers with at least $1 billion in assets. The state also has a measure on the ballot that would make a temporary tax hike permanent on individuals earning over $371,000, adjusted annually for inflation. The tax is currently set to expire in 2031.

"The impact to the muni bond market will be to make the exemption more valuable," Close said of the possible passage of tax increases. Investors will likely seek shelter in the muni market, which would send bond prices higher, he noted.

It is a different story in Florida, where voters are set to decide whether to increase the Sunshine State's homestead exemption for non-school district property taxes. Qualified homeowners currently have a $25,000 property tax exemption, as well as another for $25,000 that doesn't apply to school property taxes. The new proposal raises the latter exemption to $150,000 in 2027 and $250,000 by 2028. New residents have a five-year waiting period before receiving the benefit.

If passed, some local governments will have to find other revenue sources or increase the overall tax rate to replace what they've lost, said Tom Kozlik, head of public policy and municipal strategy at HilltopSecurities.

"It's not necessarily going to result in downgrades for sure, but there would have to be some balancing with regard to overall revenues," he said.

Not all municipalities will be hurt equally, which means credit research is important, said Close.

"Like everything in municipal finance, it is really on a case-by-case basis that you could have some credit deterioration from [a] rollback of property taxes," he said.

Other voters on Tuesday will be asked to approve more state or local government debt. That could weigh on a market that has already seen heightened supply.

Some $473.8 billion has already been issued year to date, through September, according to the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association. That's a 7.8% increase over the same period a year ago. In 2025, total muni issuance reached $587.3 billion, 14.3% higher than 2024, Sifma said.

Cooper Howard, director of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research, believes supply is the biggest issue coming out of the election.

"That amount of issuance has been one of the reasons why you have to see that strong amount of demand continue to keep pace with it for total returns to keep up," Howard said.

Fortunately, new supply doesn't necessarily come quickly into the market. "There's a window over a couple years that they can issue," he noted.

The ultimate makeup of the next Congress could also have consequences for muni investors as lawmakers sort out their priorities. Most notable is what lawmakers will do to tackle the country's affordability problem, Kozlik at Hilltop said.

"There could be some tax policy initiatives and even affordability-related initiatives that manifest themselves in the first quarter of next year," he said. "That could impact housing. It could impact higher education. It could impact public power financing."

Investors considering moves before the election may want to look at those states with tax increases on the ballot, said Close at Nuveen.

For instance, Nuveen recently began offering a separately managed account dedicated to Washington after getting an enormous number of inquiries from financial advisors. The state recently enacted a 9.9% tax on individuals with taxable annual income above $1 million, set to go into effect in 2028. But the issue is now being put to voters, who can decide whether to repeal the new law.

"At the margin, if you do think that some of these measures are going to pass, knowing that bonds usually move up with an increase in taxes, there might be some opportunities to buy ahead of the election for blue states," Close said.

Howard believes munis are attractive right now, particularly for those who are in a tax bracket of 24% or above. Fundamentals are good and the bonds largely have good credit quality, he said. "About 70% of the broad muni index is either triple-A or double-A rated."

Kozlik is also bullish, calling yields "really attractive." He suggests investors concentrate on AA-rated or higher bonds.

"The muni space is part of the portfolio that most investors want," he said. "It helps them sleep well at night."

#2848. Cowboys have a chance to respond in Indianapolis

The Cowboys suffered a painful 33-30 overtime loss to Arizona, but Sunday provides an immediate chance to respond.

Indianapolis is 2-6, has lost four straight games, and is coming off a 42-7 defeat against Jacksonville. The Colts also appear to be looking toward the future after trading away two of their best veteran players.

So basically Indianapolis is struggling badly and haven't won since Week 4. Their four-game losing streak includes defeats of 34-22, 26-20, 16-10 and 42-7. Sunday's 35-point loss to Jacksonville was their heaviest defeat in nearly four years and Jacksonville led the game 28-0 by halftime.

Dallas may be frustrated after Arizona, but Indianapolis enters this game with even less momentum. Daniel Jones keeps giving opponents opportunities and has thrown only five touchdowns to nine interceptions this season along with being sacked 22 times. Over his last three games alone, he has thrown five interceptions and been sacked 13 times.

Indianapolis ranks last in the NFL with a minus-11 turnover differential. Dallas is at minus-one. That is particularly encouraging after the Cowboys defense created two turnovers and five sacks against Arizona. Quinnen Williams had two sacks and has now recorded at least one in three straight games. DeMarvion Overshown also forced a fumble.

The Colts have also just traded away two stars so their roster is weaker than it was a week ago. They traded eight-time Pro Bowl guard Quenton Nelson to Seattle and defensive tackle DeForest Buckner to San Francisco, both moves help Dallas.

Nelson's absence removes Indianapolis' best offensive lineman just as the Cowboys pass rush finally showed some life. Buckner's departure removes a defender who had four sacks through seven games and had been one of the Colts' most disruptive players for years. As proof how important those players were to the team, Indianapolis just played its first game without both against Jacksonville.

Indianapolis has already allowed 23 touchdowns and 2,748 yards this season. Opponents are averaging 5.7 yards per play and 4.9 yards per rushing attempt against the Colts. That gives Brian Schottenheimer options.

The way Dallas lost to Arizona was incredibly frustrating, but there were signs of improvement before the fourth-quarter collapse. The Cowboys produced five sacks and two takeaways. Shemar James recorded his first NFL interception, Overshown forced a fumble, and Williams recorded two sacks. Dallas also held Arizona to only three points in the first half and carried a 14-3 lead into halftime. The challenge is making that defensive performance last four quarters.

Against a Colts offense averaging only 19 points per game and struggling badly at quarterback, Dallas has an opportunity to do exactly that.

History provides another positive. The last Cowboys-Colts game went very well for Dallas where they won 54-19 in 2022, scoring an incredible 33 points in the fourth quarter.

Prescott threw three touchdowns, while the Dallas defense forced five turnovers, including three interceptions. It was another example of what can happen when Dallas forces Indianapolis into mistakes. That's particularly relevant this week with the Colts carrying the worst turnover differential in football.

#2846. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

#2844. Todd Monken: Deshaun Watson not ruled out yet, but Shedeur Sanders preparing to start

Browns quarterback Deshaun Watson is in the concussion protocol after self-reporting symptoms on Tuesday.

Head coach Todd Monken told reporters in his Wednesday press conference that Watson hasn’t necessarily been ruled out for Sunday’s game against New Orleans. But the club is preparing Shedeur Sanders to start.

“What I would say is, credit to Deshaun — after the game, [he] felt fine. Monday night, started feeling a little bit, little bit of a headache, and came in Tuesday and, you know, reported,” Monken said, via transcript from the team. “So, credit to him for someone who really loves playing the game. And so, he is in the protocol. And Shedeur will take the reps today with the ones.”

Monken admitted that it’s “a little more difficult” for a quarterback to play on Sunday after missing practice due to a concussion. But Monken and the team will let the process play out.

“I mean, there’s a long time between now and then, right? I mean, look where the Ravens are at. And you don’t know as the week goes on what could happen,” Monken said. “It’s not ideal, right? It’s not ideal for any player to go through a week and not practice and then play. We know that. But every situation is different.”

Monken noted that Watson can participate in the team’s meetings to help him stay prepared in the event that he gets cleared. The team has been game-planning with the idea that Sanders will start. But Monken said the game plan won’t change much if it’s Sanders or Watson behind center.

“I don’t see the way [it would change], because the rest of the guys are the same, and they’re similar in some ways,” Monken said. “Some things, you know, Shedeur’s skill set is different than Deshaun’s, but still very similar in terms of what we do. We can’t change that dramatically.”

Either way, Watson is expected to still be the starter when he is cleared.

“Yeah, I didn’t assume I’d get that question. Yeah, he’s been our starter for eight games. I would assume he’s still our starter,” Monken said. “I don’t — I’m thinking about Wednesday. But I’m not — I’m really thinking about today and watching Shedeur play today.”

So far this season, Watson has completed 65.5 percent of his passes for 1,806 yards with seven touchdowns and three interceptions. He’s been sacked a league-high 33 times. His eight games this season are the most he’s played in a year for the Browns since arriving on the club in 2022.

Last season, Sanders completed 56.6 percent of his throws for 1,400 yards with seven touchdowns and 10 interceptions in eight games with seven starts.

However, here's hoping Brissett decides to let it fly again as part of this fantasy-friendly offense.

#2841. Spurs’ culture a hot topic in the mediasphere

The San Antonio Spurs' "Pounding the Rock" culture is having a moment.

In the recent 60 Minutes profile, Victor Wembanyama praised Gregg Popovich as "the greatest coach of all-time" and called the Spurs "a winner's franchise."

The reasons for Wemby's celebratory fist pump during the 2023 NBA Draft Lottery are rooted in the Spurs' culture. And the hits just keep on coming this week …

The latest episode of the Run It Back podcast featured Stan Van Gundy talking about the quiet strength and character of the Spurs' culture during a conversation about the Miami Heat's early season struggles.

Former all-star guard Mike Bibby also drew a contrast between the Spurs and his old Sacramento Kings team on a new episode of the Straight Game Podcast. At the tail end of his career, Bibby wasn't allowed around the Kings team and facility without jumping through hoops and getting approvals.

The Spurs, meanwhile, welcomed Bibby and his friends with open arms – even offering a free ride on the team plane. Co-host Kyle Draper chimed in by describing the inclusive "family" nature of Spurs' team dinners while Ty Ellis said "that word travels" around the league.

This week also saw a clip of Steve Kerr discussing Popovich's direct, but compassionate, way of delivering bad news. Kerr said he's tried to model that example in his successful coaching career.

And fast forwarding to today, a lengthy ESPN article by Michael C. Wright identified Devin Vassell as one of the modern embodiments of the Spurs' selfless culture.

Vassell said, "I don't worry about the headlines. I don't worry about points. It's just a matter of how I can impact the team."

#2840. Pleasant volleyball dominates again in OHSAA regional with Centerburg

ONTARIO — The Spartans continue to stick to the script.

For the 26th time in 26 matches in 2026, Pleasant's volleyball team won a 3-0 match, this time beating Centerburg 25-12, 25-10, 25-16 during a Division V regional semifinal Nov. 4 in the O-rena at Ontario High School.

Pleasant, ranked No. 7 in the state coaches poll for D-V, will face top-ranked Coldwater at 2 p.m. Saturday, Nov. 7 back in the O-rena. The Cavaliers (22-4) also posted a 3-0 win, topping No. 3 Huron (22-4) by scores of 25-16, 29-27, 25-13.

"They just keep gaining the momentum and the confidence, and I think they want it so badly that they aren't even willing to let a point go. I love it," Pleasant coach Kory Ute said of her team.

In a season of dominance, it might have been the most dialed-in performance of the season by the Spartans (26-0).

"This is definitely the best we've played skill-wise. We had so much energy. We were very honed in in what we were doing," Pleasant senior defensive specialist Siri Veladanda said. "We all know this is the time of season where you can't afford to make small mistakes. This is when you need to hone those skills. We knew that coming into this game."

Back at the O-rena, site of their closest set of the season when the Warriors scored 22 points in a 25-22 third-set loss, Pleasant didn't leave anything to chance. The longest run they've allowed this season was five points at Ontario, but against Centerburg (23-3), they only allowed the Trojans to peel off a 3-point run.

And with the exception of 1-0 to start the second set, the Spartans never trailed Wednesday night.

"It was the first thing we said when we were watching film. We've got to make sure we establish what we can do and do it quickly so they know," Ute said. "I don't want another team having any hope. I don't want them thinking they are going to be part of the game."

More on Pleasant volleyball: Pleasant volleyball's Kam Cocherl newest member of the Fahey Four club

In the first set, Pleasant went out to an 8-2 lead, and the Trojans called a timeout while trailing 12-5. They couldn't stop the momentum as the Spartans pushed the lead to 19-8 and 23-11 before winning 25-12.

The second set was more of the same as Pleasant built leads of 6-1, 11-4 and 17-8 before closing with an 8-2 run.

For the third set, Pleasant again rushed out to leads of 5-1, 10-4 and 12-5, but three times Centerburg — champions of the Knox Morrow Athletic Conference — cut it to three, the last being at 16-13. However, the Spartans finished it with a 9-2 run and advanced to their third straight regional championship match.

Putting together quick starts for each set was a point of emphasis for Ute.

"They have taken that seriously the last three matches. We're trying to hop on them quick, so we can make sure we can stick a fork in them," she said.

Now they will face Coldwater, the team that beat them for the Division VI state championship in 2024. Pleasant is trying to return to a third straight Final Four in volleyball.

"It's going to be not a lot of sleep and a lot of studying," Ute said

#2839. Las Vegas Raiders playoff hopes depend on the defense

It's no secret that the Las Vegas Raiders offense is putting up numbers. At the midway point of the season, the Raiders currently sit at 9th in the conference standings. With a 4-4 overall record, ranking second in total points and sixth in points per game, the Raiders can be in the playoff mix, but the defense must get better fast.

Defensive coordinator Rob Leonard must find a way to evolve or open his playbook. His Raiders defense ranks 31st in points allowed, 31st in rushing yards allowed, 30th in passing yards, 24th in passing yards per game and 30th in total yards.

Over the last five weeks of the season, the Raiders are allowing an average of 35.2 points per game. Four out of the five games they were been beaten by: the Kansas City Chiefs, the Super Bowl runner-ups New England PatriotsBecoming the 85' Bears or the 00' Ravens isn't necessary or likely to happen. Getting opponents scoring down to 25 points per game will provide the proper offset to the offense's production. Making winning much more obtainable consistently. The Buffalo Bills and the Los Angeles Rams. The good news is they were able to fly into New York and win a shootout against the Jets.

However, the defense has put some ugly football on film and in a copycat league, they have been exposed over and over again. Surrendering 822 yards rushing during that stretch and nearly 164.4 yards per game. The seven rushing touchdowns allowed aren't horrible, considering the yardage given.

Defending the pass has been an issue at the line of scrimmage and the back end of the defense alike. In the last five games, the defense has produced eight sacks and 26 hits on the quarterback. A far cry from the Hellhounds, which started the season with 10 sacks in three games.

Turnovers are supposed to come in bunches, and they most certainly did at the beginning of the season. Since week 3, however, the Raiders defense has only produced two forced fumbles and recoveries and three interceptions.

It hasn't all been bad; there are definitely things the team can hang their hats on. Raiders' defense has amassed 18 sacks and 36 tackles for loss, 6 interceptions, and 12 forced fumbles this season. Should they be able to improve on being T-2nd in turnover margin +5, T-6th in interceptions (6), T-1st In Fumbles (12), with their 13 total takeaways being 3rd best in the league, they can offset the tremendous amount of yards being given up.

Not allowing drives to extend remains the biggest opportunity to make a difference. The Raiders are 21st in 3rd down conversion (41.7%), 20th in 4th down conversion (57.1%), 5th most in defensive penalties, 6th most in penalty yards and T-4th in first downs by penalty.

Maxx Crosby ranks 3rd in the league in pressures (26), but his 3.0 sacks this year are average. Although Malcolm Koonce and Kwity Paye also have 3.0 sacks each, their pressure production of 10 and 9, respectively, leaves more to be desired.

Playing less soft zone coverage and running more twists and stunts against basic fronts will help a bunch. Simplify things for the young defensive backs; allow them to make their mistakes and learn. Resume attacking aggressively. This personnel group is much better at attacking than they are at waiting for something to happen. Good things happened when Leonard pressed issues.

Becoming the 85' Bears or the 00' Ravens isn't necessary or likely to happen. Getting opponents scoring down to 25 points per game will provide the proper offset to the offense's production. Making winning much more obtainable consistently. Lining up the Raiders to be able to capitalize on their offensive success and make a run at a wildcard berth in the second half of the season.
