#2795. How Shannon Terry Built On3 Into a Powerhouse in College Sports Media

Covering college sports is a daunting task. From high school recruiting and NIL to the transfer portal, weekly rankings and injuries, the volume of information continues to grow. As audiences demand more coverage, traditional media outlets are finding it increasingly difficult to keep pace. Shannon Terry saw an opportunity to meet that demand, using his forward-thinking approach to build On3 into a major destination for college sports news and information.

Terry is no stranger to building digital content platforms focused on amateur athletics. He co-founded Rivals in 2000 before selling the popular website to Yahoo Sports seven years later for a reported $98 million. Three years after that sale, Terry launched 247Sports, which CBS Sports acquired in 2015. However, he wasn't finished. Driven by his entrepreneurial spirit and passion for building communities around college sports, Terry launched On3 in 2021.

"I was too young to retire and felt the work I did with Rivals and 247Sports were building blocks to do something bigger and better than before," Terry explained. "On3 was significantly larger in vision and concept. I felt there was more personally and professionally that I wanted to do, and there was an opportunity to go do it. So, we did."

The strategy behind On3 differed from the approach Terry used to build his previous ventures. By combining a community-focused platform, a national media operation dedicated to breaking news and a digital content business, Terry sought to connect every corner of college athletics with audiences nationwide.

"Five years ago, we saw that college sports was going to explode. There was a conflation on everything going on, but this undercurrent of passion and community that fit what we're about," Terry said. "We didn't think a lot of media companies were really good in covering college sports. There was a lot of aggregation and blogs, but there wasn't a company that sought to own college sports media and data. We thought we could do that."

Terry and his team bet on the timing, and that decision has paid off. Five years after its launch, On3 has established itself as a major destination for college sports news, data, editorial content and community. What began with a relatively small aggregation model has evolved into a platform increasingly focused on breaking news and serving a growing audience.

That growth rests on three core principles: building a community through authentic storytelling, establishing trust as a news source and connecting athletes with the broader college sports ecosystem. On3's progress has demonstrated the potential of that approach, giving other media companies a model to consider.

"There's nothing that's going on that our news team isn't working with multiple sources on. If we can identify our audience, we don't want to compromise on our pillars. We're not perfect because it's a beast to manage this. Community, authenticity and trust. That's what we work within, but that's our mission every day," Terry said.

Although Terry doesn't consider himself a fortune teller, his vision for On3 reflects his understanding of how the internet and digital media continue to evolve. While building Rivals and 247Sports, he witnessed how audiences on Facebook could drive advertising revenue. Those experiences helped shape his approach to creating a business capable of adapting as distribution platforms and consumer habits change.

"When we built On3, we knew we couldn't be dependent on any platforms. YouTube, Facebook, Twitter, Google. We're all dependent on them to some degree, but we could build a business solely dependent on that," Terry said. "We also knew we wanted to be more than a media company. We wanted to be a platform and community, because we had 25 years of building communities already. So, we built On3 to connect that ecosystem."

Partnerships with creators and other trusted voices in college sports have become an important part of that strategy. Terry believes people develop stronger connections with other people than with the platforms that distribute their content. That philosophy influences how On3 identifies potential partners and determines whether to pursue a partnership or an exclusive arrangement.

In recent years, several established personalities and outlets have joined the On3 ecosystem. The company has partnered with Josh Pate, David Pollack and Ross Dellenger while strengthening its reporting staff with the additions of Brett McMurphy, Chris Low and Zak Herbstreit, among others.

"There are two things we look for with partners. Sheer talent is first and foremost. Does this person got it? We're not signing Pat McAfee. We don't play in that sandbox. We have to work within a sandbox of finding people to be with us for four to five years," Terry noted. "Also, passion matters. Why are you doing what you do? I've experienced the essence of college sports. Do you have that? If you have it and talent, that's powerful."

The quality of On3's work over its first five years has also attracted the attention of major networks, according to Terry. He believes those organizations will increasingly recognize the value of the grassroots communities that help define college athletics.

"This is from talking to c-suite level people. You're going to see more of an investment from the major [networks], because the fabric of college sports is the passion, pageantry, and the community. It's difficult for big brands like ESPN, CBS, and FOX to see things from the grassroots level. It's arduous to manage and build infrastructure that manages to that," Terry explained. "You will see that going forward with the networks tapping into the communities and the pageantry of college sports."

However, On3's growth has not come without some controversy. After acquiring Rivals from Yahoo Sports in the summer of 2025, Terry described the return of his former platform as "jet fuel" for On3's efforts to build a stronger athlete community. In September 2026, the company introduced Rivals Recruits, a service that allows athletes to pay a fee to claim their profiles on the Rivals website while receiving a scouting report, an evaluation grade and consideration for rankings.

According to the website, the program aims to evaluate more athletes, discover additional talent and create more opportunities. Its stated mission centers on maintaining Rivals' integrity while helping athletes who earn recognition receive the exposure they deserve.

"We wanted to give athletes education, and an opportunity to tell and control their story," Terry said of Rivals Recruits. "It's very early, but we have more than 5,000 athletes that are currently active. We are just starting this, and there are so many robust services that we are offering."

The service also offers community message boards where parents and athletes can seek information from a staff of more than 40 Rivals experts. Looking ahead, Terry hopes to expand the business by adding services for schools, potentially making those offerings available at no cost.

"We want to connect that ecosystem on our platform because we already have their eyes. Every single school lives on On3 and Rivals. Athletes and parents alike. They are all here," Terry explained. "We shouldn't rely on social media or some sass company. We should have a seat at the table at connecting that community. It's as simple and as difficult as that."

As artificial intelligence continues to reshape digital media, Terry believes On3 and Rivals are positioned to adapt. Changes in search driven by AI agents, combined with declining traffic from digital platforms, have reinforced his belief that media companies must build direct relationships with their audiences rather than rely on outside distribution channels.

Terry said On3 anticipated those challenges when developing its business plan. He views the changes as an opportunity to strengthen the company's reporting, produce more relevant content and deepen its connection with readers.

"It was written as part of our business plan. It hurts all media companies. But the positive thing about it is it's forcing us to work harder, smarter, and together. To produce better content, break more news, and be more relevant. If you are a media business built on aggregation and top of funnel audience but don't have a direct endemic audience base, you're probably done," Terry explained.

That philosophy also informs On3's position on artificial intelligence. Terry said the company will not partner with brands, creators or platforms that use AI to produce content.

"On3 is built differently and has been from the inception. We see that as an enormous opportunity moving forward, because we're seeing ad yields higher by brands that want to buy authentic audiences which we provide," Terry said. "Can AI create interesting content that can be cannibalized and hijacked? Yes, in a lot of ways. We don't use it and will not use it. If we find a fan site using it, we're done with you. We want you to read Brett McMurphy, Chris Low, and everyone associated with On3. That's important to us."

Five years after launching On3, Terry sees significant room for the company to grow. Its foundation combines original reporting, editorial content and digital media with a community designed to connect every part of the amateur sports experience. For Terry, that combination positions On3 to remain a central destination for college sports fans and the people who shape the industry.

The next chapter involves more than expanding news coverage, adding talent or developing new services. Terry wants to continue building a platform where fans, athletes, parents and schools can connect through their shared passion for college sports. Having already built and sold two successful digital sports brands, he understands the importance of adapting as the industry changes.

With On3, he believes the opportunity is even greater.

"We have an amazing platform with a lot of reach. Moving into 2027, we want to spend time and money developing talent and shows outside of editorial. That's the next evolution for On3," Terry noted. "Also, with Rivals. Let's help parents, athletes and the whole ecosystem. We're going to hit the business with everything we have, and it's exciting."

As technology changes how audiences consume information and media companies search for new ways to connect with fans, Terry is betting that authenticity, trust and community will remain essential to long-term success. His track record suggests he understands how to build businesses that evolve alongside their audiences. If On3's first five years are any indication, the company may be only beginning to realize its potential.

#2792. Justin Herbert 'was throwing deli meat' in 2-INT loss to Rams, S Quentin Lake says; Jim Harbaugh disagrees

Back in September, after Los Angeles Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert was picked off on the doorstep of the end zone in the fourth quarter of a Week 3 road loss to the Buffalo Bills, Tom Brady described the intercepted pass as a "meatball sub."

"Herbert trying to throw down the middle of the field," Brady said at the time on the Fox Sports broadcast. "But there's a safety in the middle of the field. … Poor awareness. Poor decision."

More than a month later, Herbert still hasn't snapped out his head-scratching funk, and the sandwich metaphor has resurfaced.

This time, it was used by Los Angeles Rams safety Quentin Lake, who nabbed one of Herbert's two picks last week in a 24-9 win over the Chargers at SoFi Stadium.

"I mean, he was throwing deli meat out there," Lake said on the "St. Brown Podcast," which is co-hosted by brothers Amon-Ra St. Brown and Equanimeous St. Brown.

Amon-Ra St. Brown, a four-time Pro Bowl wide receiver for the Detroit Lions, asked, "Was he?"

"Bro," Lake said prior to shaking his head and starting to smile.

Lake then began cracking up before reeling his laughter back in.

"Bro, I don't know what's going on," he continued, "but, like, we knew we were going to get a couple [interceptions]. At least one."

Herbert, the 2020 NFL Offensive Rookie of the Year and a two-time Pro Bowler since, shockingly leads the NFL with 10 picks this season.

Some of those turnovers have been straight-up flummoxing. Lake's interception came on an out route that he read like a book midway through the first quarter Sunday.

Later in the game, with the Chargers trailing the Rams 17-9 late in third, Herbert tossed the ball into heavy traffic. Cornerback Trent McDuffie easily caught it and ripped off a 26-yard return that set the stage for the Rams' final touchdown.

Coming into the 2026 campaign, Herbert had been revered throughout his young NFL career. Right from the get-go, the No. 6 overall pick thrived in the league, translating his success from Oregon to the next level.

To see him struggle the way he has this season has been jarring. Hearing Lake say that Herbert's "seeing ghosts" is comparably sobering.

When asked about Lake's comments Wednesday, Chargers head coach Jim Harbaugh disagreed with the defensive back's assessment.

"As far as anybody's opinion, I mean, I don't see that," Harbaugh said, per The Athletic's Noah Furtado. "Everybody's entitled to their opinion. That's not what I see. ... Pass game, I thought [Herbert] did his job exceptionally well. You know, two throws he would've liked to have back."

Herbert finished the game 24 of 32 with 257 yards in the air, no touchdowns and those two picks. He hasn't thrown a TD pass since an Oct. 11 matchup versus the Denver Broncos. Granted, the Chargers have had a bye week since, but that Broncos game is also the last time they've scored any kind of touchdown.

Harbaugh has frequently backed Herbert during their overlap in L.A. But it's becoming increasingly difficult to defend some of his giveaways.

After posting a completion rate north of 65% in each of his first six NFL seasons, the 28-year-old Herbert's percentage in that department has dropped to 61.8%. His sudden inefficiency and uncharacteristically high interception total have raised serious questions about his fit with first-year Chargers offensive coordinator Mike McDaniel.

The 0-7 Chargers haven't moved off McDaniel yet, however, and the ETA on Herbert's long-awaited bounce-back performance is up in the air.

Cesc Fabregas' ruthless Italian visitors ran riot at the Stade Bollaert-Delelis to record the largest away victory in the tournament's history.

Cahuzac has ordered his players to take full responsibility after Lens were torn apart on their own turf by Serie A side Como on Matchday 4. Tasos Douvikas struck a treble and set up Nico Paz as the visitors matched the competition's joint-largest winning margin of eight goals alongside Liverpool in 2007 and Real Madrid in 2015. Lucas Da Cunha, Marc Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martin Baturina and 18-year-old debutant Matteo Papaccioli also netted after an early penalty against Maxence Caqueret was overturned by VAR.

Demanding accountability from his players, he added: "We have to be able to each other in the eyes, tell each other the truth. We have to pick ourselves up and turn our attention to Sunday. Unfortunately, these things happen across the course of a career."

We were cut through and everything went so quickly. We'll get back to work quickly. We're all shocked, saddened, downbeat. That's part of life as a footballer at the top level."

Vowing to make amends in front of their home supporters this weekend, Aguilar added: "Nine goals at home, we have to accept it and shoulder it. We'll give everything when we come back to this magnificent stadium on Sunday."

Lens must now regroup quickly before welcoming domestic rivals Marseille back to their home ground in Ligue 1 on Sunday afternoon. Having seen a three-match top-flight winning streak halted by Lille prior to Wednesday's European humiliation, the squad desperately need a response before the November international break. Meanwhile, free-scoring Como have become only the second team to hit nine in a Champions League game after Bayern Munich in September 2024, taking their tally to 19 goals from four matches.

#2782. Blue Jays trade idea would bring in All-Star to get back on top of AL East after disappointing season

Blue Jays trade idea would bring in All-Star to get back on top of AL East after disappointing season originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Toronto Blue Jays enter Major League Baseball's offseason with needs on both sides of the ball, though it's fair to say their biggest priority next season is a fully healthy year and Vladimir Guerrero Jr. playing at the level everyone expects. They also have other needs, especially in starting pitching.

The Blue Jays are in a position to land a very good starter this offseason, whether through a trade or the free-agent market. The Blue Jays have a good enough farm system and have proven throughout the past decade or so that they're willing to spend with the best of them in Major League Baseball.

Among the potential options for the Blue Jays to pursue, a guy like Joe Ryan instantly comes to mind. Reports have indicated over the past two seasons now that Ryan could be had for the right price by the Minnesota Twins. It was recently floated as an option, and it could put Toronto in a different ballpark than it was a year ago when it struggled at that level.

"The Twins have flirted with trading Ryan — a control-and-command right-hander with a deep arsenal and tricky vertical approach angle — for several seasons now, never finding the right return. But the pressure's on this winter with Ryan only a season away from free agency and the Twins indicating they'll aim to refresh their major-league roster for future seasons rather than enter a prolonged rebuild.

"That opens the door for a trade involving big-leaguers on each side, one the Blue Jays could construct around any number of controllable position players such as Ernie Clement, Josh Smith, Nathan Lukes or Addison Barger," Sportsnet wrote.

The interesting part from Sportsnet is suggesting that a guy like Ernie Clement could be traded for Ryan. It feels more like the Twins would be looking for younger players, since they're not in a position to contend right now.

Ryan has been just as good as many around baseball over the past couple of seasons and impressed last season with a 3.79 ERA and 159 strikeouts in 144.2 innings.

Adding that to this Blue Jays rotation would be a very promising step in the right direction for a team trying to get back to the World Series after losing it in 2025.

“I love being a Raider. I’ve said that a million times,” Crosby said Wednesday, via video from Raider Nation Radio. “This is where I want to be. Super excited, obviously for the present, but also for what’s to come.”

The Ravens sent two first-round picks to the Raiders for Crosby. The Ravens, though, failed him on his physical and sent him back to Las Vegas, because of long-term concerns about Crosby’s surgically repaired left knee.

Crosby, 29, likely could still bring two first-round picks to the Raiders. He has 22 tackles, three sacks, a forced fumble, a pass defensed and 18 quarterback hits this season.

Crosby has played 88 percent of the team’s defensive snaps.

He expressed optimism at where the Raiders are headed short term and long term.

“I’m not thinking too far down the road. I’m being present,” Crosby said. “We’re 4-4. We’ve got a really good football team. I love this locker room. I love the guys. We have great leadership. Klint [Kubiak] is a really good coach. We have Robby [Leonard], who is a first time being a DC. He’s doing a hell of a job. We’ve got a lot of new faces, new pieces, a lot of young guys as well.

“I love where we’re going. We’ve got a lot of work to do. We know that. That’s the beauty in it. Everyone can say whatever they want. We had this conversation last week [after a 35-21 loss to the Rams], and it was like the world is ending, and now it’s all these positive vibes. For us, it’s about focus about this week, today, getting better. That’s the mindset of this whole team. I’m excited about everything.”

#2773. Lakers rookie in awe of franchise superstar’s scoring ability

SACRAMENTO — Heat superstar Giannis Antetokounmpo made his feelings on Luka Dončić clear when he referred to the Lakers superstar as possibly the greatest scorer in NBA history.

And consider the Lakers' youngest player just as impressed with how his teammate has opened the 2026-27 season.

Lakers rookie Cameron Carr is in awe of what he's observed from Luka Dončić. NBAE via Getty Images

Cameron Carr watched Dončić dominate the NBA during his combined three years in college at Tennessee and Baylor.

Now, as teammates, he's getting a firsthand look at what makes the Slovenian star special, with the 6-foot-5 wing calling the pace Dončić plays at "unique."

Luka Dončić plays at a "unique" pace in Cameron Carr's eyes. AP Photo/Jayne Kamin-Oncea

"I've said it since college, he moves in slow motion, but it just works," Carr said ahead of the Lakers' road game against the Kings on Wednesday. "I don't know how it works, but it works. He plays at his own pace; he doesn't get sped up. It's impossible to take the ball from him — I don't know how it works."

The last part of what the Lakers rookie said is an understatement.

Dončić entered Wednesday averaging a league-high 38.3 points per game to go with 8.1 assists, eight rebounds and 1.9 steals.

His 1.072 points per minute entering the matchup against the Kings would be an all-time league record, besting Wilt Chamberlain's record of 1.038 points per minute in 1961-62, if sustained for the season.

"Just to be able to be in his presence and see how his mind works every day and what he's thinking about … it sharpens his game but also sharpens people around him," Carr said. "I don't think he realizes how much impact he has on dudes like me. I'm here every day, just soaking things up. He's the best scorer in the NBA. That's big-time."

The Lakers' Cameron Carr averaged 18.9 points during his lone season with Baylor. AP Photo/Jayne Kamin-Oncea

Carr debated whether he can refer to himself as a scorer considering he just made his NBA debut during Sunday's home win over the Raptors and has played two minutes in the league.

But when he was in college, that's exactly what he was, with the former Baylor Bear averaging 18.9 points during his lone season with the program.

So he has a deeper appreciation for Dončić's scoring outburst early in games.

Dončić entered Wednesday averaging a league-high first-quarter 15 points and 22.9 first-half points.

"When you look up in the first quarter and he has 20, 22 points, you're like, 'Wow, how did he get there so fast?' That's the only question you can really ask," Carr said. "Like, he's about to make something ridiculous. Oh, he banked in a [3-pointer against the Raptors]. I ask him every game like, 'Did you mean to do that?' He like, 'Don't even ask — you know I did.'"

#2770. Sean McVay sounds alarm as Rams’ injury crisis reaches historic levels

The Rams' injury crisis has turned into a nightmare, and they've seen this movie before. Only this time, coach Sean McVay believes the sequel is much worse than the original.

Three season-ending injuries in two weeks. A revolving door to the training room. A roster that began the season with Super Bowl aspirations now looks like an ER's waiting room.

Los Angeles Rams cornerback Jaylen Watson leaves the field on a cart during the first half of an NFL football game against the Denver Broncos, Monday, Sept. 28, 2026, in Denver. AP Photo/David Zalubowski

And for McVay, the carnage has surpassed even the disastrous 2022 season, when the defending Super Bowl champions were decimated by injuries and finished 5-12, the worst record by a reigning Super Bowl champion in NFL history.

"I've been the coach for 10 years, and I know 2022 was a season that we had a lot of injuries, particularly at the offensive line, but this has been more than any year, including '22," McVay told reporters this week.

The latest casualty is cornerback Jaylen Watson, who will undergo season-ending shoulder surgery after emerging as one of the Rams' brightest offseason acquisitions.

"He's been such a stud for us," McVay said. "He wanted to be able to play through it."

Los Angeles Rams defensive tackle Poona Ford stands on the field before an NFL game against the Arizona Cardinals in Inglewood, Calif., Sunday, Jan. 4, 2026. Icon Sportswire via Getty Images

Watson joins defensive tackle Poona Ford (torn triceps) and kicker Blake Grupe (torn ACL) as the third Rams player lost for the season since they played the Cardinals on Oct. 18.

But even though those injuries have more of a long-lasting impact, it's just the tip of the iceberg for the Rams this season.

Myles Garrett underwent knee surgery. Davante Adams suffered a ruptured hamstring. Kobie Turner tore his groin. Running back Dean Connors broke his hand. Terrance Ferguson injured his ankle, Trent McDuffie battled a groin strain, Colby Parkinson has dealt with shoulder and rib problems, and Puka Nacua has fought lingering core and calf issues.

The injuries have struck every position group, leaving McVay scrambling to keep his roster competitive.

Los Angeles Rams head coach Sean McVay speaks at a news conference after an NFL football game against the Arizona Cardinals in Inglewood, Calif., Sunday, Oct. 18, 2026. AP Photo/Gregory Bull

"Guys are out at different spots that are key and critical to your identities, really in all three — on offense, on defense and on special teams," McVay said.

Yet unlike 2022, when the season collapsed beyond repair, these Rams are still fighting.

"Let's just take it a day at a time," McVay said.

Speaking of one day at a time, if there was a silver lining on Wednesday, it was the news that reigning Defensive Player of the Year Garrett returned to practice and could potentially play as soon as Sunday against the Commanders.

"It's great to be able to get him back out there. I think what we've said, just trying to stay in the moment, one day at a time," McVay said of the plan for Garrett. "As far as what that snap count looks like if he is able to play, it's really truly like, 'Let's just see how today goes, and then let's see how tomorrow goes and [then] the following day.'"

The injuries are so significant and consistent that even 49ers fans are joking on social media that the Rams might have a substation problem near their practice facility instead of the Niners.

For a team that's already exhausted its supply of healthy bodies, surviving November might be just as impressive as finishing with a winning record.

#2768. Mike Vrabel jokes about contractual obligation to meet Tom Brady ahead of Patriots reunion

Mike Vrabel has joked that he is contractually obliged to catch up with Tom Brady when the former New England Patriots quarterback returns to Gillette Stadium for their Week 9 clash with the Green Bay Packers.

Brady is set to be at the Patriots' home ground as a FOX Sports broadcaster, marking a reunion with several familiar faces from his time in New England.

However, Vrabel did not sound particularly enthusiastic when asked whether he would get the chance to meet his former teammate.

Speaking to reporters, the Patriots head coach delivered a playful response when asked about catching up with the seven-time Super Bowl champion.

"I think I'm required contractually to catch up with him at some point," Vrabel said.

He then continued the joke, adding: "I'm sure it'll be a Zoom. I'm sure they won't show up here; I'm sure it will be on Zoom."

Despite the humour, Vrabel made it clear that there was no bad blood between the pair. He described Brady as a friend and said he would "always be a friend", regardless of his professional commitments.

The two spent eight years together in New England, building a relationship during one of the most successful periods in Patriots history.

Vrabel also confirmed that they had exchanged text messages following Super Bowl LX, although they had not met in person.

Their relationship had attracted some attention after Brady said he did not "have a dog" in the Super Bowl between the Patriots and Seattle Seahawks. Some former teammates and fans were reportedly disappointed that he did not openly support New England.

However, Brady's minority ownership stake in the Las Vegas Raiders and his role as a FOX Sports broadcaster meant he had reasons to remain neutral.

Brady, meanwhile, has admitted that returning to Gillette Stadium will be an emotional experience.

"It'll be certainly emotional," he told FOX Sports' Colin Cowherd, reflecting on the memories he made at the stadium.

With Brady back in Foxborough and Vrabel preparing to welcome his former teammate, the Patriots' meeting with Green Bay will offer a reunion that goes beyond the action on the field.

#2765. Bills should be considering value of trading for player set to be a free agent

Of course I've been thinking plenty about what the Buffalo Bills might do before the NFL trade deadline.

And I've decided to transfer my stream of consciousness on this topic to my keyboard for this piece.

I'm a firm believer that in the NFL, in-season trade value is set, or at least calibrated against, the compensatory pick a player most likely would be able to fetch his original team in the future.

Of course, those compensatory picks don't arrive until the year after a player signs a new deal in following March, so the waiting game must be considered.

But for example, I'd essentially make no sense for a team to accept a fifth-round pick in a deadline deal for a veteran if said veteran could net the club a third-rounder a year down the road.

Let's use Josh Downs for example, the most recent feature in the Bills trade candidate series here at Rumblings.

Spotrac's current market value for Downs is $16.9 million per year. It projects him to sign a four-year, $67M contract in free agency next March, if he makes it there.

With Downs on an expiring contract, the team he's on the end of 2026 NFL season would likely receive a fourth-round compensatory pick (and a third-rounder isn't out of the question) in 2028 for his departure, if, of course, that club signs fewer unrestricted free agents than it loses in the 2027 free-agent cycle.

(Given the Bills are set to be $37.8M OVER the 2027 cap and currently have eight players set to be qualifying unrestricted free agents, it's a reasonably safe bet they will lose more UFAs than they sign. In 2026's free-agent cycle, Buffalo lost three qualifying UFAs and signed two.)

For the sake of the argument here, let's assume a deal for Downs in the $16M – $17M APY range would net that fourth-round compensatory selection in the 2028 NFL Draft.

That likely sets the floor regarding what the Colts would like to get in return for Downs in a trade before the deadline on November 10.

Assuming there are a few suitors for Downs, the Colts could be in search of a third-round pick for the veteran receiver.

The Bills are expected to have two fourths in the 2027 NFL Draft — their own pick and the projected compensatory pick they got for David Edwards signing a sizable deal with the Saints.

In continuing this hypothetical, the Bills would, of course, prefer to send a fourth-round pick for Downs and initially reluctant to trade away a 2027 third-round selection for him.

(again, I do not know anything in the previous sentence to be true… we are living in hypothetical land right now)

Here's why I think it'd make perfect sense for the Bills to ultimately decide to use that third-round selection for Downs (or any other trade candidate on an expiring deal):

If they did make that trade, these are the possible outcomes, within reason. And let's start by assuming Downs almost assuredly makes the Bills a better team for the second half of the 2026 regular season, and presumably, the playoffs:

After major productivity, he prices himself out of what the Bills want to pay him on a second contract, and he signs a lucrative, multi-year deal elsewhere in free agency. In this scenario, Buffalo nets a 2028 third-round (or fourth-round) compensatory pick for their "rental." The draft-pick cost to acquire him would, at worst, would be Buffalo moving down from one round to the next, and waiting a year. At best, the compensatory pick would negate the draft-pick cost to trade for him.

After major productivity, the Bills decide he's a core piece of the receiving corps who will be 26 years old before the start of the 2027 campaign and sign him to a multi-year extension. Said extension features a reasonable cap hit in the first year, a slightly higher one in the next, then finally a cap hit above his contract's APY in 2029, when he'll be 28 and D.J. Moore will be 32.

After solid-but-unspectacular productivity, the Bills let Downs walk in free agency, and net a fifth or sixth-round compensatory pick in the process. Of course, this is the least desirable outcome for the Bills and Downs. A two or three-round "drop" in the draft for the Downs rental (going from third-round pick to acquire him to, potentially, a sixth-round pick from the compensatory system).

After solid-but-unspectacular productivity, the Bills and Downs agree to a more team-friendly deal in the $10M – $14M APY range. For context, the contract Khalil Shakir signed before the 2025 campaign features $13.6M in APY. Buffalo gets a solid-but-unspectacular possession wideout entering his prime at somewhat of a bargain price.

Of course, there are other possible outcomes, including Downs playing horribly and/or getting hurt. It's impossible to predict whether or not the latter would occur, so it's not worth mentioning here.

As far as the former, given his steadiness in the NFL, fine start to 2026, and the offense he'd be entering, it's reasonably safe to project Downs making a solid impact at the very least in Buffalo.

Therefore, the worst possible outcome (within reason) for the Bills would be sending a third-round pick to the Colts and only getting a sixth-round pick in 2028 for his March 2027 departure from Buffalo.

But wouldn't amplifying the offense this season be worth, in the worst-case scenario, a possible three-round drop in the draft?

#2764. Not Hobart, not 183 vs Pak! Virat Kohli names surprising knock as his greatest ever ODI innings on his 38th birthday

Not Hobart, not 183 vs Pak! Virat Kohli names surprising knock as his greatest ever ODI innings on his 38th birthday originally appeared on Cricket News. Add Cricket News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

India ace Virat Kohli has ranked his best ODI knock, and it is not one anyone expected.

A knockout-style contest pitted his hundreds against two quieter innings, with a surprising result.

His logic reveals how he values an innings, and centuries barely feature in it.

MORE: Duffy teases Kohli, calls him 'annoying' after losing 1st ODI at Auckland

Ask most cricket fans to pick Virat Kohli's finest ODI innings and the answer would arrive within seconds, most likely the 183 against Pakistan in Mirpur or the 133 not out in Hobart.

Kohli, who turned 38 today, has chosen neither of them, and his actual pick is a good deal more modest on the scorecard than anything people would have expected.

The batter went through a "winner stays on" exercise in which his memorable innings were set against each other in pairs, and the survivor of each contest moved on to meet a fresh challenger. At the end of the process, the knock left standing was his 43 against England in the 2013 Champions Trophy final at Edgbaston.

What makes the result so striking is the company it beat. Kohli holds the all-time record for ODI hundreds with 55, yet not one of his centuries made it through to the final round, and the innings he valued most were played when a trophy was on the line.

The selection also says something about how he measures his own work. It points to a batter who rates pressure, timing and the outcome of a match above personal milestones, which is an attitude that many modern scorecards, with their emphasis on big numbers, tend to hide.

MORE: Virat Kohli strengthens reputation as ODI cricket's greatest chase master

The challenge began with a contest between the 133 not out against Sri Lanka in Hobart in 2012 and the 35 he made in the 2011 World Cup final at Mumbai. Kohli went with the World Cup knock, even though the Hobart innings involved chasing 321 in just 36.4 overs.

His 35 then held off the 85 against Australia in Chennai in the 2023 World Cup, the 122 against England in Pune in 2017 and the unbeaten 160 against South Africa in Cape Town in 2018.

It was only in the fifth round that the 43 from Edgbaston took over, and that innings then beat the 183, the 138 against South Africa and the 107 against Pakistan in Adelaide.

43 vs ENG, Edgbaston, 2013 Champions Trophy final

The final was played on 23 June 2013, and heavy rain cut it down to 20 overs per side, on a damp and tacky pitch that offered generous lateral movement. India lost Rohit Sharma early and were in trouble at 66 for 5 by the 13th over.

Kohli stepped in and top-scored for both teams with 43 off 34 balls, which included four fours and a six, at a strike rate of 126.47. He added 47 with Ravindra Jadeja, who made an unbeaten 33 off 25, and the pair dragged India to 129 for 7.

England were then held to 124 for 8, so India won by five runs and lifted the trophy. That result made MS Dhoni the first captain to win all three major ICC limited-overs trophies.

The innings that held its place for four rounds came on 2 April 2011 in Mumbai, with India chasing 275. Lasith Malinga had Virender Sehwag out for a duck in the first over and removed Sachin Tendulkar for 18, which left the Wankhede in stunned silence at 31 for 2.

The 22-year-old Kohli absorbed the pressure and hit four boundaries in his 35 off 49 balls, a strike rate of 71.42. He put together a vital 83-run stand for the third wicket with Gautam Gambhir, who made 97, before Tillakaratne Dilshan took a brilliant return catch to end his innings at 114 for 3.

The stand steadied the chase and set up Dhoni, who finished on 91 not out, and Gambhir to complete a six-wicket win and India's second ODI World Cup.

Every innings he turned down has its own place in cricket history, which makes the final choice all the more telling.

MORE: Arshdeep Singh remains the clear choice above Siraj in ODIs: Here's why

India had to chase 321 inside 40 overs for a bonus point; Kohli hit Malinga for 24 in an over and finished the job in 36.4 overs.

His career-best ODI score, made in the chase of Pakistan's 329 for 6.

A test of endurance in extreme heat, with 100 runs taken in singles, twos and threes in a total of 303 for 6.

His first match as full-time ODI captain; he added 200 with Kedar Jadhav to rescue India from 63 for 4 in a chase of 351.

It set up a 76-run win in the opener and kept India's unbeaten World Cup record against Pakistan intact.

He rescued India from 2 for 3 in a chase of 200, alongside KL Rahul's 97 not out.

A hundred in testing heat against Steyn, Morkel and Rabada that levelled the series at 2-2.

Kohli's choice will surprise many, yet it fits the kind of cricketer he has become. A man with 55 ODI centuries could easily have picked a big score, and nobody would have complained, but he looked instead at what each innings actually did for the team.

Both of his finalists came under Dhoni in ICC finals, and both ended with India lifting a global trophy. That suggests Kohli sees an innings as valuable only in proportion to what it changed, and a 43 off 34 balls that rescued a side from 66 for 5 is hard to argue against on those terms.

The pick also exposes how poorly raw numbers describe a player's influence. A 183 in a bilateral or continental game will always look bigger in the record books than a 43 in a rain-shortened final, and yet the shorter knock arguably carried far more significance on the day. Strike rates and totals are useful, but they cannot show how much a collapse hurts or how tense a dressing room feels.

There is a lesson here for younger batters as well. Kohli did not wave away his hundreds, and several of them clearly mattered to him, but he was prepared to rank them below two small scores that helped win trophies. On his 38th birthday, that sense of priorities is perhaps the best summary of his career, in which the result has nearly always counted for more than the number beside his name.

#2763. Eagles-Commanders somehow breaks 19 million viewers

When George Costanza pitched the show about nothing to NBC president Russell Dalrymple, Dalrymple asked, “Well, why am I watching it?”

More than 30 years later, that sentiment still applies. As evidenced by the fact that, per Nielsen, 19.015 million (on average) watched Sunday night’s snoozefest between the Eagles and Commanders on the network that Dalrymple once ran.

It’s an amazing number, given that it was the 2-5 Eagles against the 1-4-1 Commanders. The game itself wasn’t particularly compelling, and it followed afternoon windows that culminated in a down-to-the-wire showdown between the Chiefs and the Broncos.

The league could have flexed the game out of the evening window. It chose not to. In the end, the not-ready-for-prime-time game managed to attract far more viewers than anyone would have expected.

It helped that there wasn’t a 9:30 a.m. ET game in Week 8. Fourteen straight hours of football is a lot, even for the most ardent fan.

Then again, Commanders-Eagles was a lot. Even for the most ardent fan. And more than 19 million nevertheless watched the game.

#2760. Joel Embiid hit with technical foul for altercation in 76ers-Pistons

As far as NBA altercations go, this certainly wasn't the most intense. But Philadelphia 76ers star center Joel Embiid drew a technical foul for it, either way.

Embiid and Detroit Pistons forward Tolu Smith engaged in a brief dustup late in the first half of a Wednesday, Nov. 4 game between the two teams.

The play happened with 1:25 left to go in the second quarter, when the 76ers had the ball. Sixers forward Jaylen Brown was driving to the basket when Smith was jostling with Embiid for positioning below the basket. Smith shoved Embiid, who took exception to the move after the play was blown dead.

Embiid then gave Smith a little shove with his right elbow. The contact was minor, but it led to a quick back and forth between the two. It escalated with light shoving, and players eventually had to step in to separate Embiid and Smith, who were shouting and trying to square off.

Eventually, officials reviewed the sequence and levied a technical foul against Embiid for being the one to initiate the action with the elbow.

The Pistons crowd at Little Caesars Arena in Detroit immediately booed Embiid. Pistons All-Star guard Cade Cunningham converted the technical free throw. Embiid was seen encouraging the boos.

The 76ers went on to win 112-106, with Embiid scoring 26 points in the game.

#2759. Fantasy Football: Offenses that surged — and slumped — in NFL season's second month

Few things in life excite me more than offensive ecosystem talk. Deployment of players, weaponizing concepts and schemes; it's the best.

Figuring out the right offensive environments can also be our best path to uncovering value and ascending players in fantasy football. With eight weeks of data, we can see which teams have improved since the start of the season and which are going in the other direction. I'll spend more time on the improving teams and quickly explain why the fallers rank where they do right now. We'll use a metric I came up with to do it.

My Offensive Efficiency Score is simply the average of each team's rank across metrics I use to measure a unit's effectiveness (dropback EPA, rush EPA, points per drive, drive scoring rate, success rate and explosive play rate). Nothing special. However, the variety of variables accounts for teams that attack in different ways — teams that move the ball successfully but aren't explosive get measured the same as an inverse team.

Here's how the league stacks up after eight weeks.

Offensive efficiency scores for all 32 NFL teams through Week 8.

Quietly, plenty of interesting things are happening in the Dolphins offense. In a league full of humiliating units, this isn't one of them. Miami looks put together, well-coached and runs plenty of sound concepts.

While not flush with household names, young skill-position talents like boundary rookie receiver Caleb Douglas, slot-man Malik Washington and especially Ollie Gordon at running back have offered strong moments. The run game has taken off with Gordon at the helm, as the team has leaned into gap concepts. They rank sixth in yards per carry on gap runs and only the Patriots and Titans run it at a higher rate.

Miami has a stretch of games against the Lions, Colts, Bills and Jets defenses coming over the next month. You can play some of these Dolphins options against those weaker stop units.

After three brutal games to start the year against some of the best defenses in the league, the Patriots offense has been on fire over the past month.

That schedule caveat might be necessary but it's also fair to point out that Drake Maye has been getting it done without a stacked hand. A.J. Brown has been injured and not impactful when he's played, while other role players have stepped up. The offensive line has dealt with injuries and the run game remains middling.

Maye is back in the elite quarterback territory with his play over this recent stretch. Week 9's matchup against the Packers will be telling, especially as we assess how many of these young pass-catchers he can take with him to fantasy relevance.

The Steelers finished 31st and 30th in Weeks 1 and 2 in the offensive efficiency metric. They have ranked between fourth and 18th in every week since, with three finishes in the top eight.

The Steelers have really leaned into a bully ball mentality. From Week 5 on they use jumbo personnel (six offensive linemen) on 11.5% of their plays, the third-highest rate in the league. They slowed down their pace, pass rates and have gotten under center more often. This has led to the explosion of Jaylen Warren, who has flat-out been one of the best running backs in the NFL. Pittsburgh ranks first in rushing EPA in this span.

The passing game under Aaron Rodgers isn't quite as effective (23rd) but they do find some steady plays in the quick game and weaponize their tight ends. I enjoy watching this offense much more now than I did early in the season and it's no longer a "hands-off" environment for fantasy.

Washington has ranked between 10th and 15th in all three of their games in this span. Based on the current personnel, it's tough to imagine this team featuring a high ceiling but the floor is strong, as long as Jayden Daniels stays healthy.

That means we can at least consider the highly involved players as flex candidates in the right matchup. Antonio Williams ran the most routes on the team last week and is on track for a strong post-bye rookie bump.

The Jets attack is one of the most improved offensive units in the NFL this season, up from 31st in 2025 to 11th overall this year. They've gotten even better the last month with Braelon Allen breaking out and Omar Cooper Jr. excelling as a rookie.

The challenge for fantasy is this unit's crowded nature. They got three-deep at receiver with Cooper, Garrett Wilson and AD Mitchell, have two highly drafted tight ends and two strong backs when Breece Hall returns. You felt the consequences when Cooper failed to record a target last week, despite good film, and Mitchell went off against the Raiders.

With the unit looking viable and the defense struggling, we should get some good scripts for the Jets offense to put up points. We'll just have to try and pinpoint the right matchups.

The Texans have four top-seven weekly finishes in the efficiency metric this season. There have been some real frustrations with this unit and I have a decently long list of complaints but C.J. Stroud is playing well enough to cover up some of the sins.

The Texans need to give up on being this run-heavy unit and really lean into the passing game given Stroud's play and the receiver depth. The Texans ranked fifth, 10th and eighth in pass rate over expected from Weeks 4 to 6 but dipped down to 21st in Week 7 before their bye. How they approach this week's game coming back from their bye week will tell us a lot about whether OC Nick Caley is up for changing his stripes.

On the season, Green Bay ranks 11th in dropback EPA and 31st in rushing. That 20-point gap is tied for the second-largest in the NFL. I don't have much faith the Packers will improve on the ground. The current version of Josh Jacobs isn't enough to fix the struggling unit, despite his fantasy production, and the offensive line is the load-bearing issue.

As such, I expect this team to continue to be pass-centric and as long as they lean into that, they should be able to improve in these rankings over the course of the season.

The Bucs have gone the other way of some of the last couple of teams discussed and fully leaned into their rushing attack. Over the last two weeks, Baker Mayfield has completed seven of his 14 pass attempts. The Bucs won both games.

The Bucs have leaned into more gap runs as the season has gone on and the offensive line is responding with some excellent play. Over the last four weeks, they rank third in explosive rushing rate. Bucky Irving was one of the best running back selections this year.

Cam Ward is coming off the best game of his NFL career. That is buoying the jump in the rankings, as they were the sixth-best offense in Week 8 but no better than 19th in any other.

The Titans leaned into no huddle on 41% of their plays in Week 8, their highest rate of the season by far. This could be an aberration against the Bengals defense but I'm open to the possibility that a spread offense with a high pass rate (fifth in pass rate over expected) just suits Ward better.

The Ravens were pacing to be an elite offense early in the season. Everything has fallen apart with Lamar Jackson's injury. He looks likely to miss the rest of the season, as does his backup Tyler Huntley.

The Ravens traded for Jacoby Brissett on Wednesday, the best outcome they could hope for given the miserable situation they're in. Brissett has familiarity with the coaching staff and should pick up the offense in time to start in Week 10. He's enough to keep the ship afloat and keep Baltimore out of the league basement by the end of the season.

The Saints desperately need to get left tackle Kelvin Banks back. You can directly map their offensive collapse with his removal from the offense. Banks is back at practice this week, so his return in the next few games will go a long way toward saving this unit.

Hopefully, he can also elevate what's been one of the NFL's least effective run games. The team also desperately needs Travis Etienne Jr. to come back and be the player they paid him to be this offseason. The current stable of backs just won't cut it.

Injuries along the offensive line have sunk a unit that was one of the most enjoyable in September. They've ranked 23rd and 24th the last two weeks. Unfortunately, if they can't get an infusion of health along that line, they don't stand a chance at matching their September numbers against quality opponents.

The Falcons are unfairly penalized by a monsoon game in Tampa Bay in Week 8, where Michael Penix Jr. completely collapsed. Otherwise, they've hovered between 14th and 20th in Weeks 5 to 7. I think that's probably more appropriate for their current standing — an average NFL offense that can spike due to high-end talent in Drake London and Bijan Robinson.

Two games against the Seahawks and Broncos have set back the once-soaring Chiefs. It's also shown that if you can shut down their explosive ground game, they don't have the horses to throw on good defenses. The Chiefs need to get serious about their receiver issues for once and make a trade before the deadline. It's that simple.

You can totally ignore this one. The Bears are unfairly penalized by a 28th-place ranking in Week 5, Tyson Bagent's disaster and final start against the Packers. They've ranked 13th, seventh and fifth in Caleb Williams' three games since returning from injury. Chicago is one of the best offenses in the NFL; I have no doubt.

It feels like everyone wants to find one smoking gun that's behind the Seahawks stalling on offense but it's not that simple — despite the inclination to crush OC Brian Fleury for being unable to mimic Klint Kubiak's 2025 output. There's blame to go around.

The offensive line has been spotty but you hope that improves with Quenton Nelson settling in at left guard. The run game needs Jadarian Price to break out as a more explosive option. Sam Darnold also hasn't played well and now he's set to miss some time with an injury. Drew Lock actually captained this team's second-best offensive outing in Week 2 this year. Lastly, while Jaxon Smith-Njigba can handle an immense share of the passing volume, the lack of a true second threat has held this unit back. The extra attention he's received this year has also forced Darnold to make too many post-snap decisions, which is the biggest reason he's struggled.

Odds are, Seattle can fix one or two problems on that long list. It's not ideal that the ledger goes as deep as it does. Even if they fix a couple, I still think this tops out as an average unit overall.

The Colts' season is over. They know it; they've acted on it. I don't think 26th is their floor. If/when Jonathan Taylor and even Josh Downs are shipped off this roster over the next week, you can make a strong argument that this is the least talented offense in the NFL. I won't be looking for much fantasy viability out of anyone on this team going forward.

#2756. Suns’ Devin Booker exits Blazers game with injury scare

Suns' Devin Booker exits Blazers game with injury scare appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

PHOENIX– Following a 33-point outburst, Phoenix Suns star Devin Booker exited Wednesday's game against the Portland Trail Blazers with a concerning injury, but quickly came back.

It came during a fast-break attempt when Robert Williams III contested the shot and blocked it. The shot defense looked like Booker was hit in the face. Still, there wasn't a clear angle to see what exactly was wrong.

Coming into Wednesday, the Suns were expecting a breakthrough from their recent struggles. Luckily, Booker would be a main initiator of that.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

He had 12 points in that opening quarter, setting the stage for what would be an impressive scoring performance. The shooting was impressive, but it was getting to the free-throw line that was a major beneficiary.

Booker was in attack mode and Portland didn't have much of an answer. He was frustrating multiple Blazers defenders and continued to get to his spots.

For Phoenix, it was a painful blow, considering he scored nearly half of the team's points before the injury. It was a major sigh of relief when he returned to Wednesday's contest.

That being said, other guys had to step up to prevent deja vu from the season opener. Good news was that Miles Bridges and Jalen Green took turns leaning into being that primary scoring option.

With Booker back in the game, it might be too little too late. However, it'll be interesting if the injury is something long-term or just incidental.

Phoenix plays the Orlando Magic on Thursday, on the second leg of a back-to-back series.

#2755. 76ers star Joel Embiid gets technical foul for shoving match with Tolu Smith in win over Pistons

There were some fireworks with Joel Embiid in the first half at Little Caesars Arena on Wednesday night.

Embiid, who received a technical foul early after a shoving match under the rim, still managed to lead the Philadelphia 76ers to a 112-106 win over the Detroit Pistons. He finished with a double-double with 26 points and 15 rebounds, and powered Philadelphia to an 8-1 record on the year.

But things got heated late in the first half after Jaylen Brown was fouled while shooting under the basket. All of the sudden, as Brown was headed to the free throw line, Embiid got into a shoving match with Detroit's Tolu Smith.

Embiid was jockeying for position with Smith as Brown was working his way to the rim. After the whistle, it looked like Embiid's elbow hit Smith in the chest. Smith then swatted the ball away from Embiid. This led to a shoving match between the two, and in the kerfuffle, Embiid ended up pushing a ref away.

When the dust settled, the refs whistled only Embiid for a technical foul. The Detroit crowd rained down expletives with his last name, and the Philly center stood and egged them on, asking for more.

Pistons point guard Cade Cunningham hit the ensuing free throw from the technical, and Philadelphia went into halftime leading 56-42.

Though the 76ers led by as many as 13 in the final minutes, the Pistons surged late to make it a game. They cut it to just four points after Cunningham hit a layup with 65 seconds left in the contest, but that was as close as they got. Philadelphia held on down the stretch to secure the six-point win.

Embiid shot 8-of-18 from the field and made all 10 of his free throws in the win. The technical was his first of the season. Brown led the way with 30 points and seven assists, and LeBron James finished two assists shy of a triple-double with 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists.

Duncan Robinson led the Pistons with 27 points after he went 7-of-11 from the 3-point line. Cunningham had 26 points and 11 assists, and Ausar Thompson had 11 points and 17 rebounds. The Pistons handed the 76ers their lone loss of the season back on Oct. 25. Detroit now sits at 6-2 on the season.

The 76ers and the Pistons will end their three-game season series on February 5.

Embiid has now played in six of the team's games so far this season as the franchise continues to manage his workload to keep him healthy. While things got a bit heated on Wednesday night, Embiid still managed to lift the 76ers to the win without further incident.
