#2754. Bo Nix on the comebacks: ‘We have a lot of guys with a clutch gene’

The Denver Broncos have one 15 consecutive one score games. This is tied for the most all-time. Tying none other than Peyton Manning while he was with the Indianapolis Colts. Even more than that, Bo Nix is averaging a comeback and game winning drive at a rate far beyond anything seen in NFL history.

When asked about his success, Nix believes it goes beyond just one person. He sees the whole team having that clutch gene and that is why they've been so successful.

"I've always felt like I've wanted to feel like a clutch player," Nix said on Wednesday. "It's an important quality as a player when everybody's looking at you and the pressure's on. I don't think you do it individually. If you look at our team in the last three years, we have had 15 game-winning drives. I just think we have a lot of guys with a clutch gene. We have a lot of guys that when the game's on the line, will just find ways to go score. I'd much rather be known as the guy that wins games in the close ones than the guy that always looks great early and can win but doesn't know how to close out the game. I'd much rather be clutch than the other way around."

He's both right and wrong. You need the quarterback more than any other position to have the clutch gene. It feeds the rest. I think back to 2016 and how great that defense was and how clutch they were in 2015. Except, they were not quite clutch enough because the quarterback situation lost its mojo along the way without Peyton Manning's aura around to spark the clutch.

Tim Tebow had it. He was terrible at quarterbacking, but if the game was close everyone on that team believed clutch was going to manifest. It manifested because they all put in the plays to make it happen. It's the same with these Broncos. Bo Nix is the driver. The team is the engine. Together they clutch the heck up week in and week out.

Like John Elway's years. Like Tim Tebow's seven games. Like Peyton Manning's 2015 run. The clutch is a team game, but it needs a quarterback that the players believe can make the magic happen if given the chance.

Bo Nix is that guy too. I am enjoying this ride.

Who needs picks when your offense is lacking?

Every year that passes, I like having draft picks more and more. I fully understand that they are all essentially a roll of the dice, but I'd rather have a bunch of chances at improving the team than not. In general, I'm not normally about trading picks mid-season.

Broncos at Panthers preview: Denver aims to avoid the "trap"

The Broncos head east to face Carolina on Sunday. After a big win and brutal schedule to start, Denver can't let up.

Statistical dive into Denver Broncos record-tying 15th straight win in one-score games

The Denver Broncos have now won 15 straight regular season games that were decided by one-score (8 or fewer points). This ties the 2007-2008 Peyton Manning-led Colts. Since the start of the 2025 season the Broncos are 16 and 2 in one-score games in the regular season (1-1 in the post-season). This is a big change from 2024 when the Broncos were 1-6 in one-score games.

On preparing a gameplan for a team that recently changed defensive coordinators

"I think this: You go by all of the film you have. Really in that first quarter, there may be some things that we see that maybe have changed a little. [It's] hard to change everything in a short period of time. They have a base front and coverage they like to play and then they have a sub package and then it's really more of when they're playing it. Then us being able to in the first quarter see if there's something that's different than we've seen on film."

On if the team is managing RB J.K. Dobbins' workload

"He's a huge part of what we're doing. So each week we look at—a lot of times who's the back that's in there with the hot hand. But he's been fantastic. So to answer your question: No, that's not a hot button topic with us here. It might be with you guys, but not with us. So we're good. We're good with the rotation."

On balancing RB J.K. Dobbins' workload within a three-back rotation

"Oh, we are paying attention to, yes, absolutely. Look, it's always easy to work two (running backs) and it becomes more challenging when you're trying to work three, but he's doing a great job."

"All of that's good. The pressures can lead to interceptions, the sacks can't. So you're looking each week, what quarterback are you defending? We've seen some mobile quarterbacks periodically. Maybe you get a quarterback that you know where they're going to be in the pocket. So I think you have a rush plan based on the offense and who's playing quarterback."

On if opposing offenses have adjusted to the team's pass rush

"Certainly the timing of when you want to throw the ball. There are some quarterbacks that are… Last week, it's hard to sack [Chiefs QB] Patrick [Mahomes]. In other words, he's seen the pressures, he's seen the man… He's got a good clock in his head. So those guys that process fast are difficult. Now fortunately for us, the last play of the game, we were able to get one. I think a lot of it is the offense and the QB. To answer your question, I'm sure there's certain points of emphasis you make relative to the rhythm of your throws if you're seeing a team that rushes the passer well."

"You've definitely seen his ascension, and he's very comfortable with what they're doing. It's an explosive team. I think they're seventh or eighth in explosives. They do a great job mixing the play action, the 'RPO' (Run-Pass Option) game. They've drafted well. So all those numbers are different today than they were two years ago."

On how RB Jonah Coleman has impressed him despite missing time with an injury

"We had those glimpses in the spring and in training camp and in the preseason. Atlanta, there's a run, it just stood out to you. So the one thing that was unique about him is oftentimes you don't get to see them in third down protection plans, but he was someone that we had quite a bit of tape on. Advanced protection plans, and he's built to handle the blitz. It's one thing to know who I have, but then also to be strong enough to do it. I would say that there were three backs—we just saw one of them that we really had visions for. Each one may be a little differently, but his versatility, to your point, that doesn't always—you don't get to stamp that with a lot of rookie backs that you feel like they're going to develop into. We saw enough that we felt like he was going to be a good third down protecting back as well with the way he was built."

On if RB Jonah Coleman has exceeded his expectations in pass protection

"Yeah, he's got good ball skills. We caught him in the right pressure there. Yes, he's very comfortable. He's a different type [of] player than [RB] RJ [Harvey], but there were a lot of traits to like. More importantly, there was the exposure in an offense that sometimes the player just doesn't have that exposure, so you can't know for sure. I think we knew a little bit more with his tape."

On what allows QB Bo Nix to elevate his play when needed

"I don't know. I didn't think he struggled. There's certain…In Seattle, it was the opposite."

On QB Bo Nix's ability to raise his level of play

"I don't have the answer. I know what you're asking. I look at it internally myself like, 'All right, let's get going.' I've said this before, I'll let you know the week we don't want to start fast, but it's not this week. We want to start fast."

On what makes WR Jaylen Waddle dangerous in the low red zone

"He's someone that transitions really quickly. We catch him in a coverage, so he can start and stop and then very quickly outflank leverage, which he did on the one touchdown. I think it was fourth down, but he's got exceptional change of direction."

On the key to building momentum and stringing together wins

"Generally, you have better teams and good teams can… You can't be a yo-yo team and expect to win double digit win. You do the math. So I think what you're asking is the key to building a good football team and that would go all the way back to bringing in the right people, all those things, the right coaching staff and the right culture. Historically speaking, and I'm just going through my experience of having teams that it's hard to win two, lose–it's hard to do that and then turn around and look at the record maybe that you are wanting. Each one of the games is a different type of game. It's like that old boxing analogy. There are certain fights for certain styles and I think that applies a little bit in our game in the NFL."

"I haven't felt it until you just mentioned it. Honestly, I'm being dead serious. I know we had a big win against the Chiefs. I didn't even think how many of that is now. Yes, you're just kind of onto this next one."

"Halftime's eight minutes. We're always on the headsets talking about what's next. You get a chance for a quick message. [The] defense breaks up, offense breaks up, coaches go through real quick, go to your players. It's much quicker. It's much quicker than you could imagine. Every once in a while, there's a game where you get an extra minute, something's going on and then the Super Bowl is entirely different where you can change out your shirts and everything more like hockey. Those guys have kind of a big gap between periods. Ours is like, man… I think you just trust your instincts as to what you just have seen, what you'd like to see in the second half. We're doing this well, let's keep… Then ultimately tune out the scoreboard and just get onto the next play. It's so important. It's so important to not let one play bleed to the next and get onto the next play.

"Oftentimes we as coaches can sit there and be talking about something and it's not going to come up again. So sometimes literally getting onto the next play and knowing you can address that on Monday, or sometimes it is something that could come up again. If it's an offensive play that we've run and if we're spending a bunch of time—we'll get that cleaned up. So back to halftime. No, I'm not yelling or screaming. I had that plan I told you about before the game and the chair was too heavy."

On if a 1 p.m. ET, 11 a.m. MT kickoff changes the team's preparation

"It's new because normally it's been West, and so we'll go out Friday night, get up on their time zone, get a workout, get a little practice in. Then we're kind of going here around Noon. So whatever it is, your spark or your coffee or let's go. So fair question. We studied it and you talk to different people maybe that have been on the West Coast, and we spent time and then finally you have a win at Philly or there's been a few of those and this year we've got… We were hoping to maybe get maybe a couple back-to-back out there, stay out there. It didn't work out that way, but we're not going to Europe. We're going to Charlotte."

On T Garett Bolles' pass protection at left tackle impacting game planning

"A lot. You can't chip, nudge everyone and you want flare control into the route. A lot of times it might be one rusher that you really want to help with. Yes. I know the whole story of the blind side, but you want one of those… In other words, that left tackle, there are times where I'll say to 'Bollesy' (T Garett Bolles), 'Hey, you've got him.' He can do that. Yes, if we're having to make… Now there's times if [Charger OLB] Khalil Mack lines up there, we might move the tight end over there with a Mack protection and just nudge wherever he's at. Last week we had a little protection plan for where [Chiefs DT Chris] Jones was. So I think that's tonight's meeting and it takes a while. Yes, we appreciate the skillset he has."

On if the upcoming Bye Week factors into decisions about players returning from injury

"It's simple. Honestly it's, 'Is he ready to go?' We're going and if he's not, there's never any… Now if he's ready to go, he's ready to go. So it's one or the other. It's not, 'Well.' We don't have that luxury. Just 17 games. If he's ready, we treat it like we're playing… That doesn't factor into it at all. It's a good question."

On coordinating with General Manager George Paton at the trade deadline while preparing the gameplan

"We would meet and meet in the evening. The early part of it, all the GMs, they all talk with each other, but he and I'll visit daily. So it's not like it's consuming two hours. His group is spending a lot of time separate from the time that I'm involved with our group. In other words, they're doing their evaluation, we're doing football, but he and I daily will visit about a bunch of things."

On if "spenders" is a term being used around the trade deadline

"We just get them down there once in a while."

On if it is difficult to discuss the locker room around the trade deadline

"I always think there's buyers and sellers, right? It's probably more noticeable in the last five years than maybe in our… Or at least I notice it more. Teams that are playing well are the potential buyers. The teams maybe that aren't are the potential sellers."

On if the team views itself as a buyer or spender at the trade deadline

"Well we're a team that's playing better, so we're not looking to sell."

On if he considered kicking the chair and if doing so would aggravate his foot

"No, this is like a hundred pounds. I thought it was lighter, that's all. It was a mistake. Every chair in there is the same. So it wasn't the old… I needed the country, Western, rickety… Yes, that just blew up."

On what changes in the fourth quarter to allow him to have success in those moments

"I don't think anything changes. I just think it's opportunity, and that's just usually how the game goes. It's a one-score game at the end, and you have to go out there and win it."

On balancing winning with team and individual improvement

"Winning's the main goal as a team. There's a process that goes with it. You want to get better yourself. It's really important to win games in this league. You'll find a little bit more joy and fun in the game if you're winning. With that comes the process of playing as best as I can, getting our offense going to where we want to go. At the end of the day, winning games is what really matters."

On how the offense can stay on the field longer

"I think third-down production would help a lot. I know we're not doing great in that regard. I think that is an area that we can improve."

On how dangerous WR Jaylen Waddle is in short red zone

"I think sometimes it's part of the scheme. He gets some schemed-up plays down there. We've been repping that touchdown on fourth down (vs. Kansas City) for a really long time, so that finally got off the call sheet. It was good. You practice it a lot. You want to run it. A couple plays before, Jaylen was asking for it. He was calling for it. That's when you know you're in a good spot is when you're in tune with the coach and know what he's about to call. I just think he's getting more and more comfortable. He's playing with some confidence."

On the red zone success against the Chiefs and the importance of practicing these situations

"It shows the importance of practice and getting to those over and over. You're right, sometimes you might go through them all (plays). Sometimes you might not get to any of them because you score from farther out or don't get to the red zone like you want. The other day we experienced a lot of plays down there against a really good red-zone team. That's why you practice. That's why you carry plays and game plan. We were able to execute when it was really important."

On if he can feel when a young player like RB Jonah Coleman has 'juice'

"I think he's playing really well with confidence. He's a very intelligent young running back. What sticks out the most is he can handle a lot. You can put a lot on his plate. After we learned that [RB] R.J. Harvey wasn't going to paly last week, he got a little bit more of the bulk of R.J.'s stuff. It's impressive to see him be able to be used in a lot of different ways. When he gets in there, he's a natural runner. That's what he's drafted for. That's the position he plays. It's the out of the backfield stuff that I think is making a good impression on us right now."

On the offense's success on two-point conversions and on fourth down

"I think it's just trust in the play. Finding a completion, extending the play and not passing up one. We practiced those plays and we repped them. When it comes down to it, they called our name and they called our number, and we executed well."

On how the team refocuses after a big win

"It starts with [Head] Coach [Sean Payton]. Coach Payton is really good at that. He talks about every game in the NFL is an important game. There's no such thing as a trap game because they're all that way. They're all must-wins. If you don't play well, you're going to end up messing around and getting beat. We know the importance of this week right before the Bye [Week]. We can't go on a Bye early. This is a really important game for where we want to go. As captains and as leaders, it's just all about practice. Details have to go up a notch, as if it isn't already. More attention to detail, more communication. If you do everything a little bit more and maximize your time this week, I think we'll be in a good spot."

On how the team refocuses after a big win

"I think the main focal point is a new week, new focus, new opponent. Like I've said before, every week presents each challenge. Obviously we have a good team coming up on the road, so we know how tough that is. I tell guys all the time, it doesn't matter what you do, the past is in the past. It matters what you do now. So it's just all about getting the guys together and having that refocus and that shift."

On returning to the field last week against Kansas City

"I was very excited to go out there. I was pretty anxious the past three weeks prior to that game. It was fun to get out there with the guys and fly around, make plays. Just getting back acclimated into routine, getting that feel back in front of that crowd was amazing. That injury, it was something that just happened. It was a non-contact injury, but the positive news was it was a high ankle sprain, the best news I could get from that. So I just focused on rehab with the trainers and making sure I was able to get back fully to be able to be the best version of myself."

On Head Coach Sean Payton's 200th overall win and the winning culture he has brought to the franchise

"It's great. Obviously Sean, he's brought a winning culture to this organization. It was great seeing that. It was a great milestone for him. Like I mentioned before, he just exudes a lot of confidence, and a lot of confidence and trust in us to be able to get a job done each and every Sunday. Just for him to provide that leadership and each and every week, just preparing us for a new opponent. Just finding ways to get a win is pretty special. So that's a huge accomplishment. We were all happy for him celebrating in the locker room. It was a very special moment."

On what Head Coach Sean Payton has meant to him and how he's helped him elevate his game

"I would say Coach Payton, his first team meeting that he came in, he came in and he kind of set the foundation for his expectations of how he wanted to run the organization, the program—from the players, the behind-the-scenes people, everything. He had a plan. Watching him put that plan into effect, watching him put everything in place, the way that he holds everyone to a standard of what to expect is truly special to be a part of. For him to be the coach, leader, teacher that he is, he always says that his job and the other coaches' jobs aren't just to be coaches; they're there to be teachers. Sometimes it may be about football. Sometimes it may be about life in general. How to become a better young man, man in life. To be able to be around Coach and listen to stories, I find it very humbling and also fascinating to be able to take a step back and just listen to people's stories and the different experiences of life that they've had. For myself, I guess I've been able to play a good amount of time in the league, but Coach has been around the game almost as long as I've been alive. He's seen so many different things. I feel like for myself, to be able to have this humble perspective of this game, being able to listen to Coach talk about the different players that he's been around and coach and just experience, it's really cool to share the locker room with him and to gain as much knowledge as I can because Coach knows a lot of ball. He's been around a lot of ball. He's been around a lot of successful ball, which is why he is the sixth [-fastest] coach to eclipse the milestone he eclipsed on Sunday (200 overall wins). I'm grateful that I get to share the locker room and be around him and learn as much as I possibly can about this game and about life."

On WR Jaylen Waddle getting comfortable in the offense and his performance on Sunday against the Chiefs

"Jaylen has been busting his butt since training camp. He's been putting himself in a position to be able to allow himself and [QB] Bo [Nix] gel as well as they possibly can… Our offense has been working to get to a place of efficiency. To be able to watch him get into the end zone the past couple of times and even the two-point conversion, I think it shows the level of comfort he's [beginning to have] in our offense and us trending that direction of becoming a more efficient and consistent offense."

On what has allowed for QB Bo Nix's 15 career game-winning drives

"I think it's a confidence thing. I think it's a confidence thing in himself, a confidence thing in our offense being able to understand… I think that the more times you get thrown into a situation, the more you're able to overcome said situation. It's like a math problem. The more times you see a math problem, it becomes easier for you to solve that problem when you see it again. I think that us getting thrown into the fire and having to have these comeback moments over the past couple of years and even this year alone, I think that the more you have those opportunities, you see the success and you witness what it's like to have that success. I think there's never a game we feel like we're out of. It doesn't matter how many points we're down. We feel like we have an opportunity to come back and win. You don't want to be in those situations, but when you do have to overcome those situations, having that trump card in your back pocket of saying, 'We've been here before. We know how to win these games. Let's just go out here and do it.' I think that that allows us all to play confident and fast. It's truly special to witness and to be a part of. Like I said, you don't want to be in those situations, but being able to know you can overcome those specific spots in a game is special."

Still, not great enough to practice. Which creates the kind of mixed signals that conjure memories of the strange way in which the team handled McCaffrey’s 2024 Achilles injury, which resulted in his unexpected scratch for the regular-season opener.

Here’s the basic reality. He had a career-high 413 touches in 2025. He has 118 in seven games this year. Now 30, the workload can take a toll — especially in a 17-game season.

#2749. Philip Rivers already looking for members of potential coaching staff in NC State job twist

He doesn't yet have the head coaching job, but Philip Rivers is apparently already assembling a North Carolina State coaching staff.

USA Today reported Wednesday that Rivers "has already begun sounding out potential members for his NC State staff," with his name out there as one of the top candidates for the position.

Last week, On3 reported that the former NFL quarterback, who played four years at NC State, emerged as the focus of the Wolfpack's coaching search after the firing of longtime head coach Dave Doeren.

Philip Rivers of the Indianapolis Colts walks to the locker room during a game against the Houston Texans at NRG Stadium in Houston, Texas, Jan. 4, 2026. Getty Images

Rivers downplayed the speculation after reports emerged but didn't shut down the rumors completely.

"Never say never, but nothing imminent," Rivers said on Friday. "NC State's special to me. There's nothing there at this point. You understand the chatter is out there. Got to take it one day at a time is all I know."

Rivers' son, Gunner, a four-star quarterback prospect, has committed to NC State.

While it appeared the job was Rivers' to lose for a few days, ESPN's Adam Schefter reported Tuesday that another former Wolfpack quarterback was in the mix: Russell Wilson.

Wilson interviewed with NC State officials Tuesday, per Schefter, adding another big-name candidate to the search.

The Super Bowl champion played three seasons with the Wolfpack before transferring to Wisconsin for his senior year.

Indianapolis Colts quarterback Philip Rivers walks the sideline against the San Francisco 49ers in Indianapolis, Monday, Dec. 22, 2025. USA TODAY Network via Reuters Connect

He joined "NFL Today" on CBS as an analyst earlier this year after spending last season with the Giants.

Rivers is supposedly still the front-runner, with On3 reporting that he's meeting with Wolfpack officials on Wednesday and "remains the focus" of the school's coaching search, with a deal "expected to come together soon" despite the Wilson flirtation.

#2746. Jim Harbaugh, Justin Herbert respond to Quentin Lake's "seeing ghosts" critique

As the football-following world tries to figure out the problem(s) with Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert, Rams safety Quentin Lake provided a scathing critique of Herbert’s most recent performance, in a 24-9 loss to the Rams.

“He was throwing deli meat out there,” Lake said. “He’s seeing ghosts. I don’t know what’s going on, but we knew we were gonna get a couple. At least one.”

On Wednesday, both Chargers coach Jim Harbaugh and Herbert himself were asked about Lake’s remarks.

“As far as anybody’s opinion I don’t see that,” Harbaugh told reporters. “Everybody’s entitled to their opinion. Not what I see.”

So what did Harbaugh see in the latest loss?

“I thought he played — did his job exceptionally well,” Harbaugh said. “You know, two throws he would have liked to have back.”

Herbert said he wasn’t aware of Lake’s comments.

“I haven’t looked into it, haven’t heard anything about it, so I can’t really make a comment about it,” Herbert told reporters. “We’re really just focused on what’s going on in the building, playing football and doing what we can and doing our best to, you know, go put our best effort out on Sunday. And that’s all that really matters. Anything outside of that is out of our control.”

Still, it’s jarring to hear an NFL defender make comments about a supposed franchise quarterback that make him seem like a Jets first-round draft pick who has yet to have the game slow down. Herbert is in the seventh year of his career. He’s making $52.5 million per year.

Something has happened this year. It’s still not clear what it is, or whether it will change. Before it can change, the Chargers need to realize there’s a problem.

And that’s the most confounding part of all of it. They’ve lost seven and won none; at times, they sound like they’ve won seven and lost none.

#2745. Ravens trading for Jacoby Brissett helps 49ers keep Mac Jones in San Francisco

Ravens trading for Jacoby Brissett helps 49ers keep Mac Jones in San Francisco originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

It always felt like if the San Francisco 49ers were going to move on from Mac Jones, it would take a massive trade haul to get the deal done.

Not that Jones is one of the best quarterbacks in the world by any measure, but there's reason to believe he can come into a team dealing with a quarterback issue and help them succeed.

One of the more obvious teams in the league that could have used a player like Jones was the Baltimore Ravens, though it's now been reported that Baltimore is making a trade for Jacoby Brissett with the Arizona Cardinals.

Before this trade, one CBS writer floated the idea that the Ravens could be interested in trading for Jones, and that, for that to happen, they'd have to blow away the 49ers in a potential trade.

"It doesn't seem particularly realistic. Kyle Shanahan has said many times he wants to keep Jones, and the 49ers, like the Bears, have legitimate Super Bowl aspirations right here, right now. If the 49ers needed a spot start — or even just a few series — from Jones to win a game, it would be worth it. Unlike the Bears, though, the 49ers don't have a second legit backup. Kurtis Rourke has never played an NFL snap.

"The Ravens would likely need to blow away the 49ers to have any shot at Jones, and that simply hasn't been Baltimore GM Eric DeCosta's M.O. Instead, Bagent and Brissett seem like top options, with a flyer on a younger, potentially intriguing player also a legitimate route for the Ravens to take," CBS' Zachary Pereles wrote.

While this doesn't guarantee the 49ers won't move on from Jones, it does raise the question of which other team in the league would be interested in taking a decent-sized haul to save their season.

With Baltimore now likely out of the mix, that's one other team the 49ers can't use as leverage. The 49ers still have about a week before they need to decide on Jones, though it seems obvious now that he isn't going to get traded, barring something drastic happening.

#2742. Cavs' Jarrett Allen exits early vs. Thunder with right knee injury

Cleveland Cavaliers center Jarrett Allen left Wednesday's matchup with the Oklahoma City Thunder after suffering a right knee injury late in the first quarter.

Allen suffered the injury while setting an off-ball screen just past half-court with the Cavs trailing the host Thunder by 19 points. When Jared McCain tried to get over the screen, the Oklahoma City guard's right knee collided with Allen's right knee, which appeared to jam as he stood rooted to his spot on the floor:

The 11th-year veteran fell to the deck in evident pain, grabbing for that right knee. He laid on the floor for a moment as teammate Mario Hezonja put back an offensive rebound, then raised one finger in the air to signal for a substitution.

After Cavs guard Sam Merrill committed a foul to stop the action, Allen slowly got to his feet and walked gingerly back to the locker room. Shortly thereafter, Cleveland announced that he was doubtful to return to the contest with a right knee injury. When the Cavs came out of the locker room to start the second half, Hezonja took Allen's place in Cleveland's starting lineup alongside Donovan Mitchell, James Harden, Evan Mobley and Tyrese Proctor, whom head coach Kenny Atkinson inserted in place of the injured Peyton Watson.

Allen did not return to Wednesday's game, finishing scoreless with one rebound in seven minutes of playing time. After trailing by as many as 25, the Cavs clawed back to within two possessions several times in the closing minutes. They couldn't get all the way over the hump, though, as the Thunder held on for a 124-116 win behind 42 points and eight assists from two-time NBA MVP Shai Gilgeous-Alexander.

Allen, 28, has been Cleveland's starting center since joining the team in a trade midway through the 2020-21 season. A one-time All-Star, he's averaging 15.1 points on 63.1% shooting to go with a team-high 8.7 rebounds, 1.7 blocks and 1.4 assists in 27.9 minutes per game for the Cavs, who fell to 4-4 with the loss.

#2741. Former Steelers cornerback announces retirement

Former Steelers cornerback Arthur Maulet is retiring at 33 years old, NFL Network's Cameron Wolfe reported Wednesday evening.

Maulet was an undrafted free agent in 2017 out of Memphis who started his college career at Copiah-Lincoln Community College in Mississippi. In the NFL, the 5'9 cornerback played games for six different teams over his career.

He recorded his most games played (33) and second-most games started (eight) with the Steelers over the 2021 and 2022 seasons after recording 11 total starts with the Jets in 2019 and 2020.

Over his time in the black and gold, Maulet logged 106 total tackles, two sacks, seven tackles for loss, four passes defensed and one interception.

For his career, Maulet played in 94 regular-season games as well as five in the playoffs. After his time with the Steelers, Maulet spent two seasons with the Baltimore Ravens and one with the Detroit Lions.

#2740. Josh Jacobs: "There's a lot of stuff being said about me that's completely false"

On Friday, Wisconsin authorities released documents in the Josh Jacobs criminal case that included three allegations against him from other women. On Wednesday, Jacobs spoke directly to reporters for the first time since the material became public.

“It’s one of those things for me where it’s like, it really feels like an attack on me and who I am and my character,” Jacobs said, via Rob Demovsky of ESPN. “And at the end of the day, there’s a lot of stuff being said about me that’s completely false -- like, completely made up, and it’s like people just run with it. And I can’t change that.

“It’s just one of those things where it’s like, OK, all I can do is try to be who I am and try not to let the negative moments change who I am. And that was the biggest thing for me throughout this whole process. I didn’t want to go to a place where I felt like I was closed off and I’m angry all the time because of what’s going on in my life.”

The situation would be very different if Jacobs hadn’t pleaded no contest to a misdemeanor battery charge arising from an incident in May 2026. Admitting to an incident of violence against a woman makes it harder for him to credibly claim that three other women who contacted authorities about Jacobs doing the same thing are making it all up.

Still, Jacobs believes the newly-surfaced allegations won’t result in more missed time, following the six-game stint on paid leave arising from the most recent incident.

“With all the evidence that we have and that they have at this point, it’s not one of those things where I’m sitting back on my hands like, ‘OK, am I going to play this week?’” Jacobs said. “It’s like, I kind of already know what it is, so it’s like . . . it’s really just up to them to make their final decision. But like I’m not worried about feeling like I’m going to miss any more time than I already have.”

The league continues to investigate Jacobs, and it’s investigating the recent allegations, too. Through it all, the Packers have been linked on multiple occasions to a potential trade for Colts running back Jonathan Taylor. If the Packers land Taylor, it could mean that they’ll move on from Jacobs.

#2739. Trae Young reveals what’s ‘obviously’ been missing from Wizards’ offense

Trae Young reveals what's 'obviously' been missing from Wizards' offense appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Washington Wizards are integrating a few new pieces into the team, which explains why there are going to be growing pains for this squad. That could not have been more apparent than on Wednesday night, when the Wizards couldn't overcome some offensive woes en route to a 120-105 loss to Trae Young's former team, the Atlanta Hawks.

In particular, the Wizards could not get it going from beyond the arc. They made just six triples as a team out of 26 attempts (23.1%), while the Hawks made 19-of-49 from deep. Washington simply could not overcome that mathematical disadvantage, and fell to 4-4 on the season as a result.

After the game, Young pointed out what the obvious problem has been for the Wizards in the early goings of this season.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

"Not enough points, obviously," Young said, via Bijan Todd of Monumental Sports Network. "Our offense has been lacking, really, to be honest all season. Once our offense gets going, I definitely see us being a better team."

Through eight games, the Wizards are in the bottom-10 in the league in offensive rating, which is disappointing considering the caliber of players they have on the roster. Young certainly bears responsibility for the Wizards' subpar start on offense in this new campaign as the floor general of this squad.

The Wizards offense appears to have a hard time generating clean looks from deep, which then make it easier for opposing defenses to play them straight up. Young's production has also been much more muted compared to years past, and he has to ramp up his scoring to make this offense hum.

But at the end of the day, the Wizards will need time to figure it out. They have rookie AJ Dybantsa who's still getting his feet wet in the NBA, and head coach Brian Keefe is still tinkering with the roster to see which combinations work best. However, for the team to make good on their potential to compete for a playoff spot, there should be some urgency in that regard.

#2738. Chelsea 2-0 Manchester United PLAYER RATINGS: Who was 'sloppy' in possession? Which £70m star 'came up short'? And who is 'coming of age'?

Things continue to go from bad to worse for Michael Carrick as his prospects of holding on to his position as Manchester United manager took another dent following this 2-0 defeat at Chelsea.

United were the better team for 45 minutes but wasted their opportunities and were made to pay for it in the second half when Morgan Rogers fired Chelsea ahead.

Had no chance with Rogers' opener and Palmer's penalty. Got lucky when Caicedo hit the post in the first half, but did his best to keep United in the game and made a superb late save from Estevao.

Did well against Rogers in the first half but less so in the second as Chelsea's front line began to run United ragged. He wasn't alone in that respect.

Leny Yoro (right) started well and looked a bright spot but he faded as the game got away

Gave Rogers the freedom of Stamford Bridge to score the first goal. He had been solid up until then, apart from a slip that almost let in Neto, and got forward well at set-pieces.

Let his team down with a stupid challenge on Fofana at a corner shortly after being booked. Red card and penalty killed United's chances. A player of his experience should know better.

#2735. Nick Bosa returns to practice with limited work

49ers defensive end Nick Bosa returned to practice Wednesday after missing the team's past five games with a calf injury. Bosa was a limited participant.

"It's a long year, and I kind of just reminded myself that I need to be ready for the long haul," Bosa said, via Harrison Rich of the San Francisco Chronicle.

Bosa tore his right ACL in a Sept. 21, 2025, game against the Cardinals and underwent surgery five days later. As he was coming back this summer, Bosa experienced soreness and tendinitis in his right knee, and the 49ers backed him down until Sept. 2 when he returned to practice.

He injured his left calf while warming up in practice for Week 3.

"I must have had some type of compensation going on, especially in that first week back," Bosa said. "I was kind of trying to push it back with the [right knee] tendinitis, so I may have been favoring [the left calf] a little bit. I was just doing my normal warm-up drills one day, and I tweaked it. Calves are a pretty long process. I've never had one, so it took a little longer than I expected, but I don't even think about it anymore."

Wide receiver Mike Evans (ribs/Achilles) remained out of practice, and running back Christian McCaffrey (hamstring) also didn't practice.

Wide receiver Deebo Samuel (hamstring), safety Siran Neal (knee/foot) and offensive tackle Trent Williams (rest) were the other players who sat out Wednesday's session.

Linebacker Luke Gifford (groin), cornerback Nate Hobbs (adductor), wide receiver Christian Kirk (knee), wide receiver Demarcus Robinson (ankle), safety Marques Sigle (ankle), cornerback Upton Stout (shoulder) and linebacker Garret Wallow (hamstring) were limited.

#2734. No. 11 Maryland men’s soccer claims crucial 2-0 victory over Penn State to end the regular season

Despite being highly-ranked, No. 11 Maryland men's soccer found itself in fifth place in the Big Ten conference, looking to clinch a postseason berth with a win over Penn State – a team it had failed to defeat for years. Prior to Wednesday's match, the majority of the Terps' games this season had been decided by slim margins that prevented it from becoming consistent and comfortable.

Coming back home to Ludwig Field following a narrow road loss to No. 7 Washington on Friday, Maryland was able to secure a regular-season finale victory over Penn State with a final score of 2-0. That marked its first victory over Penn State since 2018 and its first victory by multiple goals this season.

While the Nittany Lions arrived in College Park riding momentum, having defeated Duquesne 3-1 on Oct. 28, they had nothing left to lose. Penn State was bottom of the conference standings with a record of 0-6-3 beforeWednesday's fixture.

Maryland made it clear it was looking to score and score fast in the first half. Within the first few minutes of the game, Maryland fired multiple shots on Penn State goalkeeper Justin Ross.

Relentlessly, Maryland's attack pressed on, creating an opportunity for Maryland's assist leader Henry Bernstein to pass the ball to Rodrigo Neri. The forward then found captain Albi Ndrenika, who buried a low-center goal in the seventh minute of play for his fourth score of the season.

Ndrenika's goal served as a wake-up call for the Nittany Lions, who began attacking Maryland's defense more fiercely. Regardless, Maryland showed its strength on both sides of the pitch, shutting down Penn State's playmakers before they could find their best goalscorers. It wasn't until the 19th minute that Penn State recorded its first shot on goal; that was routinely saved by Maryland goalkeeper Laurin Mack.

Penn State forward Etienne Reck made a run following a goal kick by Ross that gave him space to shoot inside Maryland's box. However, Maryland's defense blocked Reck's shot, preventing Penn State from finding its equalizer.

In the 33rd minute, Penn State delivered a corner kick that nearly resulted in a close-range goal. However, Mack instinctively dove to make the save.

Following his save, Mack initiated a counterattack, sending the ball to the left wing where Henry Bernstein delivered a cross to Dominic Ayella. But with quick reflexes, Ross stopped the thunderous shot.

The Terps continued to play with fierce intensity. The game became more physical, with the teams recording a combined 12 fouls as the first half began to wind down. With less than 30 seconds left on the clock, Ayella played a through ball for Marco Wright, who narrowly missed high, keeping the score at 1-0 to end the first half.

Coming out of halftime, Maryland continued to apply strong offensive pressure, forcing Ross to make demanding saves. Similarly, Penn State spent its first second-half trying to force its way through Maryland's defense with ball movement.

The Terps remained a threat. With one simple counterattack in the 54th minute, Maryland forward Rocket Ritarita delivered a pinpoint shot to the bottom right corner that Ross just managed to get his hand to, keeping the Nittany Lions at a one goal deficit.

Three minutes later, defender Lillian Fichelle found midfielder Zack Harris, who delivered a far-corner cross from outside the box to Ayella. His strong, bouncing header found the back of the net, doubling Maryland's lead.

Despite being close with each other on the stat sheet, Maryland firmly held its lead. But disaster struck for Maryland in the 74th minute when Ljubomir Popovic committed a foul as the last defender on a Penn State attack, resulting in a red card for the Maryland defender.

Penn State was at a clear advantage after Maryland's rejection, and because of that, it began attempting to overload the Terps' defense. But even a man down, the Terps managed to hold off the Nittany Lions to the very end, clinching a Big Ten tournament berth.

1. The offense dominated. In Wednesday's matchup, Maryland recorded 18 shots — with nine on frame — and capitalized on defensive errors that allowed it to take control of the game.

2. Popovic's suspension could be concerning. The defender has been crucial to Maryland's success, finding the goal on 40% of his shots and recording six assists in the season for the Terps. His red card suspension could create an opportunity for other teams to exploit moving forward.

3. Momentum going into the Big Ten tournament. Following the win from Wednesday's match, Maryland now looks to carry its momentum into a challenging postseason.

"We know how deep we have to dig," Ndrenika said. "…It's matured a lot of our guys, and they know how hard it's gonna be, and, yeah, we're ready for it."

#2730. Kyle Shanahan surprisingly leaves door open for possible Brandon Aiyuk 49ers return, with a catch

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan is willing to welcome Brandon Aiyuk back with open arms. Getty Images

"There's no absolutes in life, so," Shanahan said to Evan Webeck of the California Post when asked about Aiyuk potentially playing again in a 49ers uniform.

He was asked a follow-up question if he has any concern over the team trading him or releasing him, and potentially playing for a rival team like the Seahawks or Rams, and said, "No."

Aiyuk has been at war with the 49ers for the last year now due to a contract dispute that led to him firing his agent, Ryan Williams of Athletes First.

Over the summer, Aiyuk posted a video on social media calling out Williams and 49ers general manager John Lynch as "weirdos" and "creepy predators" for allegedly showing up at his door.

San Francisco 49ers wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk during team warmups. AP Photo/Jed Jacobsohn

In the video, Aiyuk said his relationship with Shanahan will never be repaired and has no plans on returning to the 49ers' team facility.

It appears that Aiyuk is eating his words and is willing to come back to the facility two days after he hired Drew Rosenhaus as his new agent — who has a plan to get him "back to work and playing again in the NFL."

Shanahan was asked if he heard anything about Aiyuk's reinstatement, and he said he has no expectation.

"I've kind of moved on with that mentally. Kind of how I talked to you guys two months ago. Nothing's really changed for me," Shanahan said.

#2727. Jamie Carragher issues apology for telling Aston Villa fans to 'shut the f*** up' - after club's owners threatened former Liverpool defender with legal action

Published: 08:57 EDT, 31 October 2026 | Updated: 11:21 EDT, 31 October 2026

Jamie Carragher has fronted up and apologised to Aston Villa and its supporters after his 'clumsy & wrong' language saw the club's owners threaten legal action.

The story took a dramatic turn on Friday night when Villa released a club statement revealing the bombshell claim that they intend to take legal action against Carragher.

In a statement on club channels carrying the name of co-owner Nassef Sawiris, Villa wrote: 'Aston Villa will initiate legal action against Jamie Carragher for his unacceptable insults about the Club and its fans.

'Aston Villa will not allow anyone to stop our ascension in English and European football. Nassef Sawiris, Chairman.'

The row, which centres on comments made by Carragher on the Overlap this week in which he pushed the idea of Unai Emery becoming the next Manchester United manager, only escalated further when Carragher doubled down, telling Villa fans to 'shut the f**k up'.

But the Liverpool legend changed his tune on Saturday afternoon in a bid to defuse an increasingly volatile situation.

I’d like to apologise to Aston Villa, the owners & their fans. My language this week was clumsy & wrong.I can assure Villa fans it was tongue in cheek but I can understand it’s not come across that way, so apologies. Good luck today UTV!

Jamie Carragher has apologised for comments he made about Aston Villa and Unai Emery

'I'd like to apologise to Aston Villa, the owners & their fans,' Carragher wrote on social media.

'My language this week was clumsy & wrong. I can assure Villa fans it was tongue in cheek but I can understand it's not come across that way, so apologies. Good luck today UTV!'

Fans were left stunned at the response by Villa to Carragher's comments, seemingly innocuous linking Emery, who is admired at Old Trafford, to the Manchester United job, which may soon be available with immense pressure on Michael Carrick.

‘I think Unai Emery is unbelievable with what he does year after year. That amount of change, players needing time to settle and he does it year after year,' Carragher had said.

'But the reason I’m going to go back at Villa fans is I can’t believe that a manager who is so successful in the Europa League, take a player, if you’d won the Europa League three or four times (Emery has won it five times) surely you’ll then want to go for the Champions League.

‘Aston Villa will never be Manchester United. I just think he didn’t do well at PSG or Arsenal, and maybe that’s because of egos in the dressing room and that sort of thing.

'But surely he would want that opportunity to manage a giant again. There is a chain in football, whether you like it or not.’

#2725. Brown puts up 30 as Sixers hang on to avenge lone loss with win over Pistons

It could have been easier, but the Sixers have defeated the Detroit Pistons for the first time since November of 2024, knocking them off 112-106 Wednesday night.

Jaylen Brown set a new season-high 30 points shooting 11-of-19 from the floor, and his biggest play was blocking Cade Cunningham's three-point attempt with 38 seconds remaining. LeBron James came close to his first triple-double as a Sixer, putting up 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists on 5-of-10 shooting.

Tyrese Maxey had his worst shooting performance of the season, put up 17 of his 19 in the second half going 5-of-16 from the floor along with seven assists. Not playing on a minutes restriction this time, Joel Embiid shot 8-of-18 for 26 points and 14 rebounds.

Cunningham led the Pistons with 26, who were once again without Jalen Durn who remains sidelined with a hamstring injury, as well as Isaiah Joe who injured his hip when these two teams last met on Oct. 25.

It was tough sledding early for Embiid and Maxey, each missing their first three field goals of the night despite some good looks. Embiid missed his 12th straight three-pointer dating back four games now, but he was able to help the offense flow in spite of that. He found a cutting Brown for the first points of the game and got his first field goal on a backdoor cut on his own.

Paul Reed had quite a start against his old club. He stayed in front of Maxey on a drive and blocked his layup attempt, and made his first two shots of the night attempting some fancy footwork. Getting one slam called back for a travel didn't sway his confidence in trying to spin around Embiid.

LeBron settled in as he began to run the offense more after the Sixers' first subs. After finding Embiid on that cut, he got aggressive with their pick-and-roll, getting each of them to the free throw line during this stretch. A steal caused by Dean Wade ending in a Dean Wade layup capped off a 10-0 Sixers run.

Brown and Maxey finished the quarter strong as the Sixers flipped the stagger with a couple minutes to go. Maxey's scoop layup were only his first points of the night, but Brown was good in all nine of his minutes. He got the basket off the dribble for an and-1 then became the latest Sixer to hit an end-of-quarter heave, putting the Sixers up eight after one.

They went right back to Brown on their first possession of the quarter and he got right to his spot in the midrange for another jumper. LeBron got an assist for that, but his impressive dimes came on his consecutive assists to Adem Bona. He waited just long enough for Reed to slide over for a quick bounce bass on the first, and the second was a left-handed laser from half court to reward Bona for getting down the floor ahead of the defense.

Cunningham started this game 2-of-8 from the floor. He also got hit in the midsection in the first quarter. Detroit's offense struggled as their head of the snake did and the Sixers were able to take advantage. James got to the basket a couple more times, becoming the first player in history to reach 16,000 career field goals in the process. Brown drilled two more threes to cap off his heater. After intercepting a pass he had a wide open layup to himself but smoked it after an uneasy takeoff. Brown said the other day he's waiting for a bit more burst to come back so hopefully this is just a blip on the radar.

A couple of turnovers to follow up that layup helped the game slowly swing the Pistons' way for the first time. After a cutting layup, Duncan Robinson drilled his third and Detroit's fourth three of the night. Maxey checked back into the game to get the offense back up and running but was subbed right back out after picking up his third foul of the night in just 10 seconds.

Embiid put back a missed layup and drew a foul in the process to end a 9-0 Sixers run, but picked up a technical foul after really getting into with reserve center Tolu Smith. It just seemed like an odd escalation from typical dead ball shooting from both parties.

That foul occurred when Brown was due for two at the line, drilling both to help offset the technical. After forcing a turnover Embiid got himself to the line getting Smith to bite on a rip through. Brown got to the line one more time and split a pair to give the Sixers a 14-point lead at halftime.

The Sixers gave Cunningham too much space as he dribbled around a screen, helping him step into an open three to start the half. He also read an Embiid outlet very well for a pick-six moments later. Edgecombe had answered with a pull-up jumper and a cutting dunk, but as Cunningham started to draw doubles, the Sixers left John Collins wide open for lobs, prompting a timeout from Nick Nurse.

Nurse went back to his hot hand coming out of the timeout and Brown drilled a contested turnaround midrange jumper. LeBron drilled a three off the catch, as did Maxey for just his second field goal of the night on the following possession. Cunningham responded by getting to the line, then taking advantage of a collapsed defense to find Robinson for another three.

As he has all year, Edgecombe was doing a great job filling the lanes and was rewarded with plenty of open dunks for doing so. He also swooped in to grab a missed Embiid layup for a big putback basket. A couple possessions later he grabbed a big rebound on the defensive end and took the ball to the hoop to help the Sixers withstand Robinson's heater.

Maxey checked back in and hit a sidestep three, a much welcomed sight after his first half. Despite the Pistons getting a pair of free throws on the ensuing possession the Sixers got up the floor quickly and found Kentavious Caldwell-Pope in the corner for a three. Brown split another pair at the line and hit another midrange J to set a new season-high for this young season. Despite some good ball pressure that led to a turnover, Edgecombe wasn't able to finish a fastbreak layup, keeping the Sixers at a 16-point lead.

Once again the Sixers started their quarter going to Brown and once again he was able to rise up and knock one down inside the arc. With Embiid having played 24 minutes and Jabari Walker at the scorer's table it wasn't clear if Embiid's night was over, and Detroit was picking up momentum going to the basket. After four possessions ended in three layups, Nurse called a timeout and put Embiid back in the game. Bona didn't just sub out but went to the locker room after taking a hit to the head during his shift.

Kevin Huerter drilled a three coming out of that timeout continuing the pursuit, but Embiid was able to knockdown a jumper to settle things down. Maxey drove his way to the line and LeBron was able to find KCP near the hoop for a basket. They surrendered a fast break that led to another Pistons three, but Reed picking up his fifth foul of the night for a thin Detroit backcourt and Maxey being left alone for another three on the left wing put the Sixers in a good spot with four and a half to go.

After missing a three badly at the end of the shotclock, Maxey appeared to make up for it running out after a miss, but he smoked the fast break layup as well. Brown fouled Robinson attempting a three on the following possession for a big swing.

Robinson made two of the three after a timeout. The Sixers went to the Maxey/Embiid two-man game and a pocket pass set the big fella up for a much needed midrange jumper, but they couldn't keep Robinson down for too long as he buried another three coming off a screen. LeBron had Edgecombe set up for a rolling layup down the lane but Edgecombe fumbled the pass, giving up another easy open basket at the rim for Philadelphia.

Capping off a messy night of officiating, Cunningham got a pair at the line on a play he either should have had an and-1 or the foul be on the floor. Regardless, he cut the lead to six. LeBron followed it with a poor jumper behind the block that didn't go and Cunningham got right back to the basket to cut it to a four-point game.

The Sixers dribbled out their next possession and the ball swung to Embiid behind the perimeter with the shotclock winding down, but he was unable to bust out of his long range slump. It was fitting that Brown made the biggest play of the night, blocking Cunningham's pull-up three-point attempt on the next trip down the floor. It set the Sixers up to win the game from the free throw line and summed up in one play why the Sixers beat the Pistons for the first time in their last eight tries.

The Sixers were able to win the game at the line, but they might have picked up a considerable bump or bruise to worry about. As he was trying to avoid turning the ball over, Maxey came up favoring his right hand as Detroit was fouling him. He's dealt with finger injuries on that hand in each of the last two seasons. Maxey was at least able to hit four straight free throws to help the Sixers pull out the win.

#2724. DeVonta Smith still sidelined with injury

Philadelphia Eagles WR DeVonta Smith (hamstring) did not participate in practice Wednesday, Nov. 4.

Why It Matters: Smith has been sidelined for several weeks because of the injury and will likely miss another game if he does not practice at some point this week. Fantasy players can monitor the situation but should not count on Smith to play in Week 9.

#2722. Lisandro Martinez's self-destruction sums up a broken Man United as Michael Carrick's future hangs by a thread - and there are still questions to ask of Xabi Alonso's Chelsea, writes IAN HERBERT

Published: 10:52 EDT, 31 October 2026 | Updated: 12:01 EDT, 31 October 2026

The sun was high and fierce and Michael Carrick shielded his eyes from it on the few occasions he got to his feet and tried to direct the Manchester United players, who did not seem to hear him. The United contingent hurled personal abuse at Sir Jim Ratcliffe and Jason Wilcox. The scrutiny was remorseless and unyielding.

For a time, there seemed to be hope - and no one can say that this latest hammer blow for Carrick removes the doubts about Xabi Alonso’s methods – but it was crushed by the same fecklessness and mindlessness that is burying United alive.

Carrick did not cast so much as a glance at Lisandro Martinez - who had just finished off the team’s hopes by leaping with Wesley Fofana and extending an elbow into his face to concede a penalty - when he walked past on the way to the tunnel.

Bruno Fernandes and some others made a half-hearted protest about the dismissal but Carrick didn’t. He knew. This looked for all the world like a footballer looking to leave the field early – making no attempt to play the ball as he was up with Fofana, having been booked for a reckless challenge on Morgan Rogers, just three minutes earlier.

The Argentinian, whose words publicly challenging the club a few weeks back will always belong to the story of these Carrick-era death throes, will perhaps want to trash talk the club again when he has been shown the door. But it was also his own gross inadequacy which had allowed Morgan Rogers to dance through the defence to put Chelsea ahead. Martinez should never darken the door of a United squad again.

Michael Carrick’s future at Manchester United looks increasingly untenable after his side imploded in the second half to lose 2-0 against Chelsea at Stamford Bridge

Lisandro Martinez, publicly outspoken earlier this month, was booked twice inside two minutes

There are few grounds for optimism from here but Fernandes is one. When he forfeited the chance to write a new script for himself – blasting into the stand after Bryan Mbeumo’s cross had gifted him a golden opportunity – the prospect of him righting the wrongs of the past week seemed forlorn.

By then, he had already been dumped on his backside by Romeo Lavia and Moises Caicedo and ceded possession. But he brushed off the mockery of the Chelsea faithful to put in a performance which demonstrated that he has a part to play at United.

In front of the small United away section before the game, he went through a free-kick taking routine, arcing one into the top-left corner and striking another against the bar.

He was the one who saw the picture late in the first half, sliding in a pass which sent Amad in. When United went 2-0 down, he conferred with Carrick. He lingered before United’s fans at the end and they accepted him. The turmoil remains but he will be needed.

Fernandes was the one who saw the picture late in the first half, sliding in a pass which sent Amad in and United went 2-0 down, he conferred with Carrick

There can be no overstating the gravity of the week just passed for Xabi Alonso: a lamentable performance in defeat to Tottenham, the Carabao Cup defeat to Liverpool and the Spaniard’s suggestion that trophy didn’t matter, which has gone down badly with the fans.

His brief programme notes made it clear how much he wanted something better here but the yawning gaps between Chelsea’s midfield and defensive lines and the ease with which United exploited them in the first half were disturbing. Wesley Fofana was lamentable.

United’s Amad could have plundered them twice – first burgling Fofana on the half-way line but lacking the pace to accelerate on and score, then easing into space and failing to despatch when one-on-one with Mike Penders. Cole Palmer’s error allowed Matheus Cunha to send Fernandes off but Mbeumo couldn’t connect with his cross. United’s failure to capitalise was fateful.

Wesley Fofana was lamentable and United’s Amad Diallo could have plundered them twice

As both sides squandered chances and laboured under basic defensive challenges, it was hard to countenance the notion that two gladiators of European football used to compete in this fixture and actually contested a Champions League final.

The occasion cried out for a moment of class and it was Morgan Rogers who provided it, taking a ball Romeo Lavia had won, dancing his way through half of the Manchester United team and slipping the ball home.

The defending shamed the traditions of the visiting team. Carlos Baleba, Andrey Santos, Martinez and Harry Maguire just watched Morgan go. But it was Rogers, taking his Chelsea tally to six goals and four assists in all competitions, who turned the game around.

Morgan Rogers had scored a brilliant solo goal for Chelsea's second-half opener to show up United’s awful finishing

It was always clear that the test of United’s mettle would come if they fell behind. The general view of the fans marching through Euston Station at 11am was that if United scored first they would come away with the points. If they conceded, their heads would drop and the psychological failure at the heart of this team would come to the fore. And so it came to pass.

In a straw poll of 15 or so at Euston, only two thought Carrick was the man to restore ‘Standards’, to quote the mantra captured on an Old Trafford banner last week. The way Chelsea turned the screw just bore that pessimism. Cole Palmer converted the penalty Martinez had conceded to finish things off.

It was to the jubilant cheers of the Chelsea faithful that Estevao left the field towards the end and this was an afternoon which will live long in the memory for him. Twice he had the ball in United’s net – pouncing on Senne Lammens' failure to hold a cross in the first half and taking Palmer’s pass to crash home in the second – but twice he was denied by VAR.

By the end, he had the control of Chelsea’s right as he interlinked with Palmer and came close a third time. Some will say this calls into question Alonso’s decision to limit his game time so far, though it was only as United fell away to nothing that he ran the show.

Some will say Estevao's display calls into question Alonso’s decision to limit his game time so far, though it was only as United fell away to nothing that he ran the show

As the minutes ticked down on another desperate result for United, the away section sang Carrick’s name once more and, significantly, he walked over towards them alone once more, just as he had after the defeat at home to Bournemouth. But it was a less emphatic assertion of empathy and support for him this time, and he did not linger so long.

The talk of Eddie Howe’s availability grows. It emerged on Saturday that under the terms of his departure agreement with Newcastle United that he will cost United only £3million. The United fans are really not convinced by him and it looks like the club will work through many more managers before a corner is turned. The constant will be that support base, who began and ended the match singing Sir Matt Busby’s name. They will always have their history.
